# Campaign verification checklist — living data file

**As of:** 2026-08-13 · last campaign scored: cloud `02bd4445` (BHU observer, 120 monthly saves 1936.2–1946.1, unbranched). Build = **`313633035`** (repo HEAD), proven by `wa_tlm_version = 4` and the full `wa_tlm_nav_*` family present **in the first save** plus linear git ancestry `88e516780` → `a2744825d` → `fde1ede1e` → `e3629b3f0` → `313633035`; the run window (18:34:47–19:47:09) opens 2m43s after the last commit. **Fixes 46–52 are therefore all IN and every FAILED below is a genuine behavioural failure, not a build artefact.**
**Headline, the war: Germany WINS. This is not a stall and not a run-out-of-saves failure — the Wehrmacht retakes Paris in 1945 and finishes the campaign with 90 controlled states, 250 divisions at 97% equipment fill, Berlin, and not one of its 30 starting core states ever lost.** The western front collapses between **1945.8 and 1945.9**: 13 metropolitan French states flip in ONE month with no battle behind it (Allied divisions in France 130 → 121, German 132 → 123); Allied-held French states run 21/24 (1945.8) → **8** (1945.9) → 3 (1945.12, still 3 at 1946.1). **Root cause, established and reversible: the posture equipment gate is a cliff.** Posture level 1 and level 2 both gate on `WA_AI_fielded_eq_ratio > @WA_AI_POSTURE_MIN_EQ` (0.9), and both consumers (`WA_AI_MILITARY_ALLIES_exec_vs_germany`, `_grind_vs_germany`) carry `abort_when_not_enabled = yes`. ENG's ratio went **0.947 (1945.8) → 0.887 (1945.9)**, `posture_vs_ger` fell 2 → 0, and every `front_control … execute_order = yes` the western Allies held against Germany was retracted at once; the unordered front was then rolled up at **−105 Allied divisions over four months against −9 German**. USA followed at 1945.12 (`posture_vs_ger` 1 → 0). ENG recovered to 0.915 by 1946.1, posture returned to 2, and Allied divisions in France ticked back up 39 → 56 — **the reversibility is what identifies the gate rather than the fighting as the cause.** Aggravating standing condition: the Luftwaffe held ~2:1 over France for the whole liberation (9,716 vs 6,624 planes at 1945.6; 12,285 vs 5,530 at 1945.9), and enemy air supremacy vetoes posture level 1 outright, so ENG was riding the level-2 grind alone with no fallback under it. **Three French states were NEVER Allied-controlled in 21 monthly samples** — 17 Franche-Comté and 28 Alsace-Lorraine (strategic region 19 Central France) and 32 Isère (region 20 Southern France); regions 19 and 290 Western France receive `front_unit_request` but **no `front_control` with `execute_order = yes` from any Allied block** (the only candidate, `WA_AI_MILITARY_COUNTRY_CAN_FRONT.txt:119-135`, is `execute_order = no`, and Canada's army reaches 0 divisions by 1945.11). Region coverage is not the whole story: state 32 sits inside covered region 20, and at 1945.8 **Alsace-Lorraine was held by ZERO German divisions while 138 Allied divisions stood in France** — enveloped-pocket handling is a separate defect from the coverage gap. State 29 Nord-Pas-de-Calais is owned AND controlled by **RBE**, not GER, so tag-scoped `front_control` never covered it either. Second finding of the campaign: **Fix 51's `production_upgrade_desire_offset` lever produced zero behavioural change** — that diagnosis is owned by R30/R33/R35 and was worked in a separate session (see the equipment headline below). Third: **Fix 52 partially worked** — the USA held 1-2 north-corridor regions at every sample 1942.6-1945.6 against zero pre-fix, and every corridor region held was held by an escort task force (owned by R36).
Headline, equipment stack: **the shared root cause proposed after `bec4d829` was wrong twice over, and the obsolescence theory that replaced it is also dead.** Fix 51 moved the whole package to `production_upgrade_desire_offset` (the production-line layer) and R30/R33/R35 still fail. A follow-up hypothesis — that the wanted equipment was excluded because it carried `obsolete=yes` while the unwanted type was its chain's sole non-obsolete member, fixable by re-parenting the equipment graph to match the branching technology graph — was tested against the saves **before** any file was edited, and **both halves are false**: (a) `obsolete` is not an archetype-uniqueness rule (288 (creator, archetype) groups hold 2+ non-obsolete members, up to six; JAP `jap_cruiser_submarine_hull_2` is non-obsolete while the newer `_3`/`_4`/`_5` are obsolete), and (b) it does not gate production at all — **7.0% of lines and 9.6% of assigned factories run obsolete-flagged equipment**, including USA's 68-factory `tank_usa_medium_chassis_3` line, and by the final save USA's `tank_usa_medium_chassis_6` is *itself* obsolete while carrying six lines and 439 factories. **The re-parenting change was NOT made; it would have fixed nothing.** See `wa-lessons-learned` 2026-08-13.
**What this campaign therefore establishes about the levers, negatively:** the design layer (Fixes 46/48/50) does not steer line selection, and the production-line layer (Fix 51) did not either — but **whether Fix 51's blocks ever armed is not observable from a savegame**: `production_upgrade_desire_offset` is not serialized into a country's `ai` section (proven with three ungated controls, incl. the long-standing `SOV_dont_build_shit_guns`, all equally invisible). The gates were open when it mattered (USA carried `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_tun_small_ok` at 1943.1, so the `+100 _4_2 / −100 _5 / −100 _6` block should have been live) and USA still ran 48 factories on `_5` and zero on any Sherman. The remaining untested lever is **research suppression**, which is also the only one Expert AI relies on.
**A third, independent defect is now measured and is sufficient on its own to explain ENG:** the `ai_equipment` design layer **does not cover the equipment the AI actually builds**. A new coverage audit (`tools/equipment_evaluator` → `output/coverage_gaps.md`) finds **39 chassis researchable from inside a role that no design in any of that country's groups describes, 11 of them in a branched role** — including all four of ENG's post-Cromwell mediums (`tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` Comet, `_7`, `_8`, `_9`). With no design to match, the engine auto-designs and builds them, and the mod's ranking, its `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_*` resource gates and its emitted `production_upgrade_desire_offset` blocks all miss them. This is why `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_ENG_TANKS.txt` emits a single `+100` on the Cromwell with no suppressions at all: the generator ranked 7 designs and the AI was building an 8th it had never heard of.
**Superseded As of (2026-08-12) · campaign `bec4d829`** (BHU observer, 123 monthly saves 1936.2–1946.4, `dlcs=257535`, HOI4 1.19.2, unbranched). Build = **`88e516780`**, the full equipment stack — `wa_tlm_version = 3` is present **in the very first save**, and since the v3 bump and equipment Fixes 46–50 ship in that one commit, the build is all-or-nothing and Fix 50 is IN. (The preceding campaign `a8b29e09` ran with those same files already in the working tree and read `wa_tlm_version = 2`, which proves the cloud box deploys **committed** state — never reason about a cloud build from local file mtimes or the local `hoi4.exe` start time.)
Headline: **the best war arc yet scored, and a total failure of the equipment stack.** F5 PASSES for the first time since `9be92c89` — `hitler_has_died` 1945.9.25 then `ww2_has_ended` 1945.10.2, GER collapsing 57 → 30 → 22 → **0** controlled states with the post-war redistribution executing as designed. F1 lands **+1 day** off historical, F2 to the day, F7 is the earliest SCW resolution yet, and the `a8b29e09` USA division treadmill **did not recur** (15 → 177, monotonic). Against that, **R30, R31, R32, R33 and R35 all FAIL on their first real test.**
**The five equipment failures share one root cause: Fixes 46/48/50 edited the WRONG LAYER.** `ai_equipment` `priority` governs the **design layer** — vanilla `common/ai_equipment/_documentation.md:36` states it is the priority "for creating a design in this group or for creating or upgrading equipment to use one of these designs". It does **not** decide which equipment type an existing production line runs, nor whether that line converts to a newer type. That is arbitrated by the `ai_strategy` type **`production_upgrade_desire_offset`** (id = an equipment *type*; vanilla's own comment at `common/ai_strategy/SOV.txt:359` reads "100 essentially means we don't require a stockpile surplus"). Expert AI 5.0 uses it **46 times, at `value = -100`, precisely to keep the AI producing an older type** (`EAI_PRODUCTION_equipment_strategies.txt:11-52` holds lines on `infantry_equipment_2` until the stockpile reaches 10 000). A second engine behaviour explains R31's missing variant: the AI does not create a second design on an archetype it already has a design for — EAI gates its groups on `has_design_based_on` to exploit exactly that. **Earlier note in this file that "priority is evaluated downstream of engine obsolescence" was wrong and is retracted**; `obsolete=yes` is a symptom of the line having already converted, not the cause. Do not re-tune any priority factor — the levers are `production_upgrade_desire_offset`, research suppression, and `mark_older_equipment_obsolete = no` on scripted `create_equipment_variant`.
**Previous campaign `a8b29e09` (2026-08-12), headline retained for its still-open diagnoses:** **the air-basing fix package works mechanically — all six of R8's Fix 46/47 probes PASS — and the Eastern Front is won a second time, with Berlin falling 1946.6.12.** R8: `_started` collapses from 115-184 to 20 at a **55% yield vs ≤28% pre-fix** (the queue-growth guard is live), the USA is **not** locked out (7/8 samples funding, `started` 14→38), ENG funds air **and** keeps refineries (40 factories vs **1** last campaign), the override sits at 0.5 and self-releases, cross-builder double-booking is **gone**, and slots are genuinely un-shared (ENG holds 3×tag-20 **and** 2×tag-21 simultaneously, no stale railway tag anywhere). ENG planes/capacity reaches **1.64×**, the best recorded. R27 reproduces cleanly (`hold_the_line` releases at 1942.6 as `fielded_eq_ratio` crosses 0.9; SOV 250→478 divisions, schwerpunkt ending in **Upper Austria**), R34 PASSES on its first test (Allied divisions in France never return to 0 — against 69→0 last campaign), R13 PASSES for the **first time ever** (Cyrenaica flips at ~11 months against a ≤12 bar, then changes hands six more times), and **R7b PASSES after three FAILEDs** with an exact tier signature (ITA holds a single `ger_inf_4` entry of exactly 6 000).
**F5 still FAILS — but it is now a run-out-of-saves failure, not a stall, and the west is the only thing still broken.** `fall_of_berlin_sov` **1946.6.12** is the last global flag, so the campaign ends **0 months dark** (vs 21 and 20 in the last two). GER is uncapitulated only because the saves stop: capital relocated to Hessen, 29 controlled states down from 79, 129 divisions against SOV's 478, all six German puppets capitulated. The Normandy beachhead **held** this time (state 15 flips at 1944.7 and is still FRA at 1946.7; Paris liberated 1944.6.27 and never lost) — and then the western front **froze at 11 German-held French states for 23 months**.
**The lead suspect has moved again, and it is now R10, not R15/R8.** The USA never exceeds **53 divisions** in ten game-years (48–53 from 1942.6 to 1945.9, then down to 37) while sitting on 130 376 idle rifles and a 440k unspent conscription pool. Previous campaigns crossed 100 divisions in early 1944. **There was no army to push inland with** — which explains a frozen front far better than basing does. **A same-session follow-up refined this and is recorded in R10: the USA is not idle-blocked, it is on a treadmill** — 263 divisions raised and 243 destroyed 1942-46, a loss rate ~143 %/yr in 1944 against ENG's ~24 %/yr, so the 48-53 band is the trough of a sawtooth. A user hypothesis that German submarines caused this was tested and **falsified** (ENG took more convoy damage and grew; USA manpower and equipment both accumulated) — the destruction is land combat. The question for the next fix cycle is **why the USA loses divisions 5-6x faster than the UK**, not why it never deploys, and the basing numbers improved this campaign anyway (USAAF in the UK 449 pre-landing then **900 → 1 252** post-landing, first-ever non-zero USA aircraft in France, siting finally in the bomber counties). R15 nonetheless FAILS **by 0.1 points** (24.9% vs a ≥25% bar) — settle the Canada/Newfoundland scoring convention before scoring it a fifth time, because that choice moves the number ~10 points.
Six items retire this campaign (R5, R7a, R21, R22, R23, R25) and **R19 is held at 3/3 rather than retired** — its criterion (4) has never been computable from a save and the queue-wide synchronised stall-counter reset (SOV 14 slots all at 19, JAP 17 all at 23, GER 9 all at 16) means criterion (1) may be measuring an artefact rather than the fix. Still failing: R8/R15 (outcome only), R9 (A* success zero for the 4th campaign — but note ENG's counter pins at *exactly* 100 across 60 saves, which looks like a `< 100` cap rather than a gate going false), R10, R17 (Sicily taken for the first time at the ≥12 bar, then **recaptured**; the fix's own sub-probe is clean), R24, R28, R29, F6, F8.

**Build state for the NEXT campaign — the front/posture stack (2026-08-13, after `02bd4445`).** Two fixes were authored in this session and are in the working tree, uncommitted at the time of writing. **Fix-number collision RESOLVED 2026-08-13: R36's naval work keeps `Fix 53` (53a/53b); the posture hysteresis was renumbered to `Fix 55` here and in `WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_effects.txt`. No bare `Fix 53` outside R36's naval scope remains.**
- **Fix 55 (posture hysteresis)** — `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_effects.txt`. New constant `@WA_AI_POSTURE_MIN_EQ_HOLD = 0.85` beside the existing `@WA_AI_POSTURE_MIN_EQ = 0.9`. Entering an offensive posture still costs 0.9; **keeping one already held costs only 0.85**, applied to both level 1 and level 2 via an `OR` on `check_variable = { WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_vs_@PREV > 0 }` in `ROOT` scope. Brace balance verified (238/238 → 246/246, +8 both sides). No new instrumentation. Scored by the new item **R37**.
- **Fix 54 (tag-free front execution)** — `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_MILITARY_FACTION_{ALLIES,AXIS,CHINA_FRONT}_FRONT.txt` plus two new triggers in `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_triggers.txt`. The six exec/grind `front_control` blocks no longer name an enemy tag: `enable` now reads `WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_has_execute_target` / `_has_grind_target`, and the payload targets a `country_trigger` on the per-enemy verdict. Covers German puppets, covers regions 19/290 without a region list, works ahistorically. Brace balance verified on all four files. **Deliberate widening to state:** a faction member now also executes against non-principal enemies where the posture says battles are winnable (ENG vs JAP, say), which the tag-scoped version never did. A `country_trigger` parse probe was boot-tested this session and came back clean — nested scope, `@PREV` substitution, `OR`/`AND` compounds and a scripted-trigger call all parse. **But parsing is not resolving:** the probe blocks were `always = no`, so `@PREV` was lexed and never evaluated. Whether `ROOT` still means the strategy owner inside a `country_trigger` is **UNPROVEN**, and settling it is leg 1 of the new item **R38**.
- **Fix 56 (AIFC needs an army, not just territory)** — one term added to `WA_AI_AIFC_should_select_sector` in `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_AIFC_triggers.txt`, plus the mirrored constant in `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_AIFC_core.txt`: `@WA_AI_AIFC_MIN_DIV = 12`, applied as `has_army_size = { size > @WA_AI_AIFC_MIN_DIV }`. Every prior eligibility term measured territory, so Reichskommissariats with 9 states and **zero divisions** held schwerpunkt corridors — 10 of 28 sector holders at 1944.9 could not have executed one. **Performance fix, not behavioural**: the wasted sectors were verified inert. No new construct class (the same `has_army_size = { size > @CONSTANT }` form already sits 15 lines below in the same trigger). Brace balance verified. Scored by the new item **R39**. Threshold 12 is a user decision and is tighter than the measured `> 4` blast radius — see R39's threshold-choice note.
- **NEW TELEMETRY, no fix attached — the `WA_TLM_post_*` family (WA_TLM v5 → v6).** Written per (country, enemy, week) at the posture calculus in `WA_AI_MILITARY_posture_effects.txt`, **not** by the monthly sampler. It exists to answer one open design question: **posture level 1 has no exchange-rate veto** (`_post_xr_signal = -1` is tested only in the level-2 limit), so a country with the numbers at the contact line keeps ordering balanced attacks however badly the trade goes, until its `fielded_eq_ratio` collapses. That was established by reading the code and has **never been measured** — the scored campaigns are AI-vs-AI, where nobody deliberately fortifies a line, and `_post_xr` is a temp that has never been serialized (the save-native substitute, `war_relation` casualty ledgers, is stored one-sided and already produced one confidently wrong 13:1 reading). **Next analysis session: read `savegame.py tlm <TAG> <last save> --match "^wa_tlm_post_"` for GER/SOV/ENG/USA/JAP and report `post_exec_xr_lt100` / `_lt50` / `_lt25` as shares of `post_exec_xr_n`.** The buckets are cumulative and nested. If `lt100` is near zero the defect is theoretical and no fix is warranted; if the mass sits below 0.5 it says where a level-1 veto threshold belongs. **Do not design the fix before that reading** — the naive version (adding the existing veto to level 1) is known-bad three ways: `@WA_AI_POSTURE_XR_VETO = 0.25` is derived from the grind's 4× manpower premise and does not transfer; the casualty deltas are global per country, so a multi-front enemy's losses elsewhere inflate the ratio and the veto would be weakest exactly in the common case; and because the levels are an `if`/`else_if`, a level-1 veto falls straight through to the level-2 veto and lands on posture 0, i.e. the same hard cliff Fix 55 was written to remove. The shape that survives review is *demote level 1 to a careful level-2 push*, not *veto*. Owes an F9 boot test with the rest of this stack.
- **Stage 2 is deliberately NOT done.** Collapsing the three faction pairs into one Default-layer pair was considered and rejected for now: `front_control` is "Exclusive per area, last-set wins per mode, Country > Faction > Default", so moving the exec blocks to Default would put them in the same layer as the emergency stops (`EXEC_no_stockpiles_stop` priority 10000, `EXEC_low_equipment_hold` priority 500) and make resolution file-order dependent. Resolve that explicitly before attempting it.

**Build state for the NEXT campaign (2026-08-13, after `02bd4445`):** the equipment generalisation stays **SUSPENDED**, and this campaign narrows the remaining levers rather than adding one. Settled negatively and not to be revisited: the design layer (`ai_equipment` priority), the production-line layer (`production_upgrade_desire_offset`, tested by Fix 51 and still failing), and **engine obsolescence** (not an archetype rule, not a production gate — the proposed equipment re-parenting was tested against saves and NOT made). What is now measured and actionable **without** another campaign: the `ai_equipment` **coverage gap** — 39 researchable chassis with no design in any group of their country, 11 in branched roles, listed in `tools/equipment_evaluator/output/coverage_gaps.md` by the new audit. ENG's medium role is the worst case (all four post-Cromwell chassis uncovered) and closing it means **authoring the missing designs so the evaluator can rank them**, not suppressing them — nothing there has been scored, so a blanket `-100` would be an unevaluated guess. **And the production-line lever is now settled negatively too, from documentation rather than another campaign:** vanilla's own comment on `production_upgrade_desire_offset` (`common/ai_strategy/SOV.txt:360` in the game install) reads *"100 essentially means we don't require a stockpile surplus"*. It offsets the desire to **upgrade an existing line**, gating on the stockpile surplus that upgrade waits for. So `+100` on an older/wanted design is a **no-op** (you never upgrade *to* an older chassis — roughly half of Fix 51's entries could never fire), and the type never sees **line creation**, which is the actual failure shape (USA opens *new* T23 lines; SOV opens *new* Malyutka lines). Expert AI 5.0's 46 uses all fit the real semantics — each `-100` is gated on `has_equipment = { … < N }` and holds an existing line on the old rifle until the stockpile fills. `equipment_variant_production_factor` is not the alternative either: vanilla only ever gives it an **archetype** id, so it cannot discriminate two chassis of one archetype. Untested lever remaining: **research suppression**. Of the three generator defects listed after `bec4d829`, **two were already fixed by Fix 51 (`a2744825d`) and are closed**: the `SOV_heavy_tanks` floor now emits `0.000009` for T 35 (monotone by rank: 10 / 3 / 1 / 0.3 / 0.1 / … — the `9e-06` scientific-notation bug is fixed in `emit.py::_pdx_num`, though note values below ~rank 5 may round to 0 in-engine), and ENG `medium_tank_4` (Crusader Mk I) now carries `tank_eng_medium_chassis_3`, not the Covenanter's `_2`. The third (the Comet) is the coverage gap above. **Fix 39 is EXONERATED** — see R32; the accusation rested on reading `resource@X` as the net column, and the real effective aluminium was +8.3 against a `> 50` bar. Still open and unowned: **`fall_of_berlin_sov` never fires on the scripted-defeat path** despite GER reaching 0 controlled states.

**Superseded build state (2026-08-12, after `bec4d829`):** no new fix has been authored. The equipment generalisation is **suspended and needs re-targeting, not re-tuning** — the remaining 407 operations and the 18 blocked cases must not be applied, and the infantry/artillery/vehicle deployment must not start, because the generator emits `ai_equipment` `priority.factor` edits and that is the wrong layer (see the header). The replacement levers, in order of confidence: (1) `ai_strategy` `production_upgrade_desire_offset` at negative values on the equipment types we do not want lines converting to — stock vanilla, used by vanilla CHI/ENG/GER/SOV and by Expert AI 46 times; (2) **research suppression** — don't research what we won't build, which is Expert AI's primary method and fixes USA mediums and JAP subs for free (SOV Malyutka is a 1936 START tech and is NOT fixable this way; the ENG Lancaster leg is blocked by two focus prerequisites); (3) `mark_older_equipment_obsolete = no` on scripted `create_equipment_variant`, the one direct anti-obsolescence token, which Expert AI never exercises. Validate whichever is chosen with a minimal isolated test, not another 10-year campaign. Three generator defects found by this scoring are fixable independently of that question and should land first: the inverted `SOV_heavy_tanks` floor (T 35 at `factor = 9` above IS 8/IS 4/IS 3), the Comet (`tank_eng_medium_chassis_5`) that no `ai_equipment` file references at all despite ENG building it with 30 factories, and the duplicated `tank_eng_medium_chassis_2` on `ENG_tank.txt` `medium_tank_3`/`medium_tank_4`. Two separable findings also want their own items: **Fix 39's `WA_AI_industry_overextended` guard nails ENG's resource latches shut permanently** (`alu_large_n = 0` all campaign while net aluminium ran +185 to +976/day against a `> 50` bar; SOV's same gate opened at n = 75), and **`fall_of_berlin_sov` never fires on the scripted-defeat path** despite GER reaching 0 controlled states.

The design rationale behind all three lives in `documentation/WA_REFINERY_CONTROLLER_REVIEW.md`, and **§1 of that document must be read before touching this system** — the functional need inverts what a code-only reading concludes.

Changes are four numeric `factor` values in JAP, one in GER, one `ship_radar` token added to an existing `allowed_modules` list, and comments; brace balance verified on both files (delta 0). **No new instrumentation, no boot test owed** — R33 is answered from save-native production lines and fleet composition, same as R30.
And **Fix 49** (2026-08-12), which closes the role bypass Fix 47 left open — `ENG_planes.txt` group `ENG_strategic_bomber` now carries the same latched aluminium gate as `ENG_heavy_strategic_bomber`, so the cap is a *role* cap rather than a Lancaster cap. Folded into **R32** (widened, not split — see the note in that item); no new item and **no new instrumentation** (it reuses the Fix 47 latches, `wa_tlm_version` stays 3, which also means TLM cannot fingerprint it — use git ancestry). Changes are five priority modifiers, one deferred supersession, one hard cutoff, and one added design block (`strategic_bomber_2_lr`, the long-range Stirling) using only constructs Fix 47 already introduced in this file plus a compound `enable = { OR = { … } }` of the kind `ENG_naval.txt` already ships; brace balance verified (delta 0) and `tools/equipment_evaluator` re-parses the file with 0 errors. The Fix 47 **KNOWN UNKNOWN carried by R31 applies to this fix too** — nobody has yet confirmed in-game that `ai_equipment` `priority` modifiers re-evaluate scripted triggers at all. Fix 49 doubles the exposure to that question rather than resolving it; if R31's design leg reads pre-fix behaviour, R32's role leg will too, and the finding is the shared mechanism, not two gates.

**New item opened 2026-08-13, and a fix authored the same session: R36 (the AI parks its escorts) + Fix 52.** WA's three-part Atlantic escort plan in `WA_AI_NAVAL_FACTION_ALLIES.txt` excluded ENG, USA and GER from its own `enable` via a trigger that is a plain tag list, leaving the plan to CAN/RAJ/AST/NZL — 27 hulls at 1942.6 — while the USN's four escort task forces sat in the Gulf of Aden, the Solomon Sea, the Algerian coast and the central NE Pacific. Fix 52 deletes the exclusion in all four affected blocks (three Allied, one Comintern). It carries **no telemetry fingerprint** — confirm by git ancestry — and its own pass leg (leg 0, corridor coverage) is what tells you whether `naval_dominance` steers fleet-to-region assignment at all.

**Diagnostic detail behind it:** A user report — "a large part of the escorts stay in port" — reproduced on `bec4d829` at 65-88% of the ENG and USA navies idle in wartime and the USN at **zero** convoy escorts in 1946.4 with Japanese submarines still raiding. Mechanism established (missions are a fleet-level property; region-less fleets are 100% idle and hold the entire idle mass), cause not yet localised to a WA lever — see R36's KNOWN UNKNOWN. Shipped with it: `savegame.py navy` (the mission split is invisible to PDXScript, so it can only come from the save) and the **WA_TLM v4 `nav_*` family** covering the outcome side, which owes an F9 boot check on three unverified tokens.

Protocol for scoring, retiring, and adding items: see `../SKILL.md`. Streak = consecutive PASSED; FAILED resets it to 0; `N/A (DLC)` / `NOT CHECKED` leave it untouched.

**Tooling note (2026-08-11, no campaign scored):** `savegame.py` gained four commands — `army`, `resources`, `buildings`, `decisions` — that replace the hand-rolled scans F8/R5/R10/R20/R25/R29 used to describe. The probe lines below were rewired to them. Two process changes shipped with it: `../SKILL.md` step 2 now requires `Probe:` lines to be quoted **verbatim** into extraction prompts (the paraphrase that dropped R10's `units` scoping contaminated F8/R5/R10 in one scoring), and `documentation/WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §2.1 is now the semantics registry for the legacy `*_dbg_*` counters.

Trajectory plausible for a 1946 white-peace as in `9be92c89`; unresolvable on a truncated run. If later saves of this campaign arrive, re-score.

User-diagnosed proximate cause at 1944.7: the D-Day beachhead has no logistics (Normandy hubs at 18–31 demand vs 5 capacity, territory Free-France-owned) — see R9's dead supply-line construction. Caveat: `9be92c89`'s white-peace chain fired 1946.9, 3 months past this campaign's end, but there the Allies were advancing; here it is a two-year stalemate.

Originally-German states held by SOV: **0**. By the Allies: **0**. `d_day_happened` 1944.6.7 and the landing succeeded (Normandy taken, Paris liberated 1944.6.16, Mulberries placed — R22), but after `BAKU_SABOTAGED` 1944.8.8 there is **not one substantive global flag in 20 game-months**. R9 remains dead but is no longer the lead suspect — this beachhead did not starve.

GER is nonetheless **uncapitulated** at 1946.3 (no depth-2 `capitulated=` anywhere in the save), stability 0.80 / war support 0.996, holding **30/30** originally-German states and its capital, **23 of 29** originally-French (Allies 5, Paris still German) and 12 of 26 Polish. Originally-German states held by SOV: **0**; by the Allies: **0**. Last substantive global flag is `d_day_happened` **1944.6.7** — **21 months dark**. **The new mechanism is the beachhead reversal, not a stall:** the landing worked (Mulberries on spec per R22, Nero capped per R21, 33 Allied divisions on states 15+1016 at 1944.8.1 against a designed 33 per R18), then state 15 Lower Normandy flipped GER→FRA 1944.6→7, held through August, and was **German again at 1944.9.1**; 29 Nord-Pas-de-Calais reversed the same way. Decisive asymmetry: Luftwaffe basing in France **952 → 4 349 planes between 1944.6.1 and 1944.7.1**, USA land-based aircraft in France at 1944.7 and 1944.8 = **zero**. Route the diagnosis to R15/R8 (Allied basing), not to R9 or R27.
  - 2026-08-12 · `a8b29e09` · **FAILED — but the best F5 reading in six campaigns, and it fails on the finish line rather than on a stall.** **Berlin fell**: `fall_of_berlin_sov` **1946.6.12**, state 64 is SOV-controlled at the end, and GER's capital has **relocated to state 55 Hessen** (first occurrence across scored campaigns). Terminal state: GER 29 controlled states (collapse curve 79 → 67 → 64 → 50 → 43 → **29** over 1945.6→1946.7), 129 divisions vs SOV 478, stability 0.525 / war support 0.964. Originally-German states GER 14 / SOV 16 / Allies **0**; originally-Polish SOV 24 / POL 2 / GER 0; originally-French FRA 13 / GER 11. Last substantive global flag is `fall_of_berlin_sov` itself — **0 months dark** (vs 21 on `9a4cd657`, 20 on `911bed3c`). **This is a run-out-of-saves failure, not a stalemate.** The `9a4cd657` beachhead reversal did NOT recur: D-Day 1944.6.8.5, `liberation_of_paris` 1944.6.27.9, state 15 Lower Normandy flips GER→FRA at 1944.7 and is **still FRA at 1946.7**. But the western front then froze — German-held French states 23 → 16 → 11 (1944.8) and **static at 11 for 23 months**; state 29 Nord-Pas-de-Calais stayed RBE and the northern beachhead never opened. **The war was won entirely in the east.** The new lead suspect for the frozen west is **R10**, not R15: the USA never exceeds **53 divisions** in ten game-years (48–53 from 1942.6 to 1945.9, then down to 37–40) — there was no army to push inland with.

- **Pass:** the Pacific war reaches a termination path (Japanese surrender chain).

- **Streak:** 0
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-10 · `9be92c89` · FAILED — Japan frozen at 1942 extent through 1946.12; no termination mechanism fired (by design gap).
  - 2026-08-10 · `66d6b53c` · FAILED (known gap) — JAP undiminished at 1945.6 (units block larger than GER's); the Pacific contributes zero global flags after `fall_of_singapore` 1942.4.28 — 38 months dark.

## RETIREABLE — fix verifications

Delete an item when its streak reaches its threshold (3 = narrow probe, 5 = behavioural).

**Causal chains (added 2026-08-11 — score mechanism fingerprints per item, but each chain's behavioural OUTCOME is scored ONCE by its owner and cited by the others; the USAAF case proved that outcome-level failures cascade through hidden dependencies):**
- **USAAF-Europe chain:** demand ledger (R15, `124b2a3b6`) → capacity funding (R23) + construction (R8, R16) → allocation (R26 lane) → **outcome owned by R15** (≥25% overseas + UK basing pre-D-Day).
- **ENG-economy chain:** gates floored (R23) → refinery strategies reachable/sited (R25) → MIC brake (R24) → lane funding (R26) → **outcome owned by R24 criterion 2** (mil plateau + UK-isles IC off the 161 flatline + import decline).
- **Mediterranean chain (UNBLOCKED 2026-08-11):** the "R9 blocks R13/R17" dependency is **retired**. `911bed3c` falsified it — R9's A* is still dead everywhere, yet the Normandy beachhead worked fine (ports capped per R21, Mulberries placed and dismantled per R22, Paris liberated in 10 days). Dead supply-line construction does not by itself freeze a theatre, so R13 and R17 may now be diagnosed on their own merits.
- **Eastern-front chain (new, owns F5 this campaign):** R27 (`surrender_progress` used as a capability proxy) → offensive veto from ~Sep 1941 + AIFC hard-off from ~Dec 1941 + sector lost 1942.9 + theatre-air-base/refinery/PC-floor lockouts → **outcome owned by F5**. R1 scores only the age cycle on countries that HAVE a sector; R27 owns everything a losing-but-capable country is denied. Note R16 and R25 are *also* downstream for such a country — do not score SOV's absence from those as their own failures.

### R7b. Lend-lease relief — infantry-equipment leg (recalibrated)

- **Fix under test:** `2d48a1a17` (35k/6k surplus, 4k support starving, common-enemy pairs).
- **Pass:** ROM/HUN rifle stockpiles off zero while GER holds surplus.
- **Probe:** recipient `production` → `equipments` infantry-equipment totals + `creator="GER"` share (same registry-resolution and ledger caveat as R7a).
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural).
- **Streak:** 2
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-10 · `66d6b53c` · PASSED — the recalibrated gate demonstrably fired: HUN GER-created rifles 10 → 6 975 between 1943.6 and 1943.9 with GER at 41 764 (over the new 34 999 gate, under 49 999 → base 6 000/pull — a delivery the old 60k gate would never have allowed). Caveat: the leg opens only in brief donor-surplus windows (GER above gate at 2/8 samples; in 1944 donor and recipients starve together and the leg is correctly gated off).
  - 2026-08-11 · `31eaf7e6` · NOT CHECKED — the mechanism never ran: GER cleared the >34 999 donor gate at exactly one sample (1943.9, 36 062 — of which only 17.3k GER-created; the gate opened on captured war booty) while neither recipient was starving (<10k) on that date; on the dates ROM/HUN do starve (1944.6, 1945.3) GER sits below the gate. Zero GER-created rifles delivered all campaign. Finding for a follow-up: the donor/recipient gate windows never overlap for GER, and `has_equipment` reads a stockpile inflated by captures.
  - 2026-08-11 · `911bed3c` · **FAILED — the never-overlapping-windows failure is now confirmed structural, not campaign variance.** Donor gate (>34 999) and recipient-starving bar (<10 000) coincide at ZERO of four anchors. Two near-misses bracket the problem precisely: at 1943.6 GER holds **34 616 — 383 rifles short** of the gate while ROM/HUN sit at 13.2k/11.4k; at 1945.6 GER holds **102 499** (3× the gate) while both recipients sit just *over* the starving bar at 12.2k/12.6k. Upgrade from last campaign: not literally zero — HUN gained **2 407 GER-created rifles** between 1942.6 and 1943.6, so one delivery burst did fire. ROM never got more than 38. **Two calibration findings for the fix:** (a) the absolute donor gate and absolute starving bar are tuned so tightly that a 383-unit miss kills a year of relief — make one or both relative to army size / recipient deficit; (b) the same surplus is R5's 116.8k hoard, so this is one defect with two symptoms, not two.
  - 2026-08-12 · `9a4cd657` · **FAILED — and the diagnosis has genuinely moved: the windows DID overlap once, and still nothing shipped.** A full 122-month sweep finds GER clearing the >34 999 donor gate in **14 months** (1940.7–1940.9 and 1945.1–1945.11). The only month where that coincides with a starving, at-war, non-capitulated ally is **1945.1 — GER 39 421 rifles vs HUN 4 137** (ROM/BUL/ITA all 12.3–14.8k, above the 10 000 bar). HUN then rose 4 137→18 282 by 1945.2, but its GER-created rifle stock went **0→4** — the gain was its own production, so no relief transfer fired in the one valid window (minimum tier is 6 000). Campaign-wide maxima of GER-created rifles in recipients: ITA 596, ROM 375, HUN 7, BUL 1 — **zero relief-scale deliveries in 122 months**. The 1940.7–1940.9 overlaps are correctly refused (ROM/BUL at 0 rifles, but neither at war). GER's own peak is **101 033 at 1945.6**, so donor wealth is again not the binding constraint.
  - **Next step is instrumentation, not another threshold re-tune (2026-08-12).** R7a fires on the *same donor/recipient pairs in the same weeks*, so the shared pair test is sound and the infantry branch is failing on something specific to it. Prime suspect inside `WA_AI_LEND_LEASE_request_surplus_relief`: `random_other_country` picks **one** donor per recipient per week with the `limit` evaluated on the candidate, so a recipient can repeatedly draw a non-qualifying country and silently do nothing. Add a counter on *donor selected* and *infantry branch entered* before the next fix attempt — three campaigns have now been spent inferring this from stockpile deltas.
  - 2026-08-12 · `a8b29e09` · **PASSED — reverses three consecutive FAILEDs, with an exact tier signature.** Relief-scale GER-created rifles are demonstrably delivered, and GER's vanilla lend-lease ledger is all zeros, so these are `send_equipment` transfers as designed: **ITA 1943.3 holds a single `ger_inf_4` entry of exactly 6 000** (the base `send_equipment` tier) after sitting at 9 403 the month before with GER crossing the 34 999 gate mid-month; **HUN 1942.12 `ger_inf_3` = 8 723** (HUN 8 370 at 1942.11, GER 33 904 and rising); **BUL 1945.1 `ger_inf_4` = 7 406** (BUL 10 448 at 1944.12, GER 43 948). Campaign-wide maxima of GER-created rifles: HUN **8 723**, BUL **7 406**, ITA **6 000**, ROM 2 491 — against 7 / 1 / 596 / 375 last campaign. GER clears the >34 999 donor gate in **11 of 126 months** (1940.8–1940.9, 1944.5, 1944.12, 1945.1–1945.7; peak 95 805 at 1945.6). **The unreliability is still real and still worth instrumenting:** two snapshot-level overlaps produced nothing (1944.5 GER 38 302 vs HUN 9 263; 1945.6 GER 95 805 vs ROM 9 933), while three deliveries landed in months that did not read as overlaps — GER's own stock swings violently between weekly pulses (support 29 995 → 7 035 → 29 333 across 1943.7–1943.9), so monthly snapshots straddle the pulse. This is consistent with the `random_other_country` one-donor-per-week lottery hypothesis; **the donor-selected / infantry-branch-entered counter is still the right next step even with the item passing.** ROM is the weak recipient on both legs and never received a relief-scale drop before the collapse. **Cross-reference correction: the two legs are NOT disjoint in this campaign** — every infantry delivery lands in a month where the support drip is simultaneously live on the same pair, contradicting the `9a4cd657` finding.
  - 2026-08-13 · `02bd4445` · **PASSED — the best reading of any campaign.** Relief-scale GER-created rifles land in **ROM 16,903 (1945.4)**, **HUN 15,701 (1945.6)**, **BUL 19,971 (1944.9)** and ITA 7,142 (1940.10) — against prior maxima of 8,723 / 7,406 / 2,491. GER clears the >34,999 donor gate in **23 of 92 monthly samples** (1942.8, 1942.11, then continuously 1944.5→1946.1; peak **135,006** at 1944.10), and ROM/HUN stockpiles are non-zero at every one of those months. The window-overlap problem that dominated three FAILEDs is gone at this donor wealth.

### R8. UK air hosting works

- **Fix under test:** `24ffda1ac` (level-ladder rewrite) + instrumentation `57d6136dd` + `124b2a3b6` (demand side: `Allies_dday_air` pre-arms 1944.2.1 with `Allies_dont_logi_strike_during_bob` standing down in step — +200k on region 239 live from February, so southern-England capacity finally has a pull to fill). New sub-probe: USAAF wings based in the UK at a **1944.3–1944.5 save** (pre-landing window), not just at 1944.6.
- **Fixes 46–47 (added 2026-08-12) — the throughput levers this item's own 2026-08-11 audit named.** That audit ranked three levers; **(1) and (3) are now shipped, (2) is NOT — and (2) is the likeliest remaining binding constraint.**
  - **Fix 46** — `WA_AI_PC_has_same_type_project_here` was ROOT-keyed, so for `allied_build` types the host and its ally each saw an empty array for the other. On `9a4cd657` at 1944.7, **ENG and USA were each funding an air base in state 126 AND state 860 simultaneously**, and the hosting-capacity gate counted the same state twice as claimed. Now backed by a builder-agnostic per-state/per-type counter `WA_AI_PC_state_type_projects^<type>` (incremented in `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, decremented in `WA_AI_PC_end_project_by_id`, both through the same `var:WA_AI_PC_target_state` accessor). Also added the free-slot test to both allied branches, which previously could fund a build that `WA_AI_PC_complete_project_by_id` then refused to spawn.
  - **Fix 47** — air bases move to a new **priority band 300** (above the 250 strategic refineries, below rail-prewar 500) while `WA_AI_PC_air_force_needs_basing_priority` holds (own air force > 500 planes, or a faction partner > 1000); `@UK_AIR_HOSTED_FACTION_SHARE` 0.5 → **0.65** (target 133 levels vs a structural ceiling of 132); the USA branch's host-condition gate **removed entirely** so the ally co-builds until the target is met; and ENG takes `WA_AI_PC_override_max_factories_factor = 0.5` while the deficit stands, lifting its PC pool from ~37 to ~53.
  - **Regression this sequence nearly shipped, and the reason the two USA probes below are mandatory:** the band-300 change alone would have *closed* the USA's old `air_factories_assigned < 1` gate permanently (ENG now always assigns something), locking out the builder that supplied **585 of 874 air civ-months** measured over 1942–44. Score the USA's participation explicitly; do not infer it from total capacity growth.
  - **Lever (2) now shipped too — all three are in.** `queued_amount_` counted **all** type-2 projects country-wide, so the UK strategy shared its 3-slot budget with R16's theatre strategy (`911bed3c`: both ENG slots held Pacific projects at 1943.6, all three USA slots non-UK at 1945.6), and band 300 could not arbitrate because UK and theatre air bases sit in the *same* band. `WA_AI_PC_start_project` now takes an opt-in `_project_queue_max_scoped`; when set, `queued_amount_` counts only projects carrying the caller's `_project_type_id` (UK = 20, theatre = 21). Each strategy gets its own 3 slots. Unscoped callers are bit-identical to before.
  - **Two shared-temp traps handled in that change, both worth knowing before touching this path again.** (i) `_project_queue_max_scoped` is latched and zeroed at entry to `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, because **30 of the 35 call sites never set it** — the same class of bug `queue_functions.txt:681-689` records for `_project_queue_max`. (ii) `_project_type_id` is deliberately **NOT** cleared the same way because `WA_AI_PC_start_project` still consumes it for the scoped budget and persists it on the new project; the two air strategies retire their own private tags caller-side. **Fix 50 closes the adjacent railway leak:** the production wrapper now pins stale-project validation to type 13 even when a frontier-port helper leaves the shared temp at 14. The deterministic `WA_TEST_RW_014` harness test reproduces that precondition and requires the stale rail to be cancelled while the port survives.
- **Pass** (ownership recut 2026-08-11 — R8 owns the CAPACITY side of the USAAF-Europe chain; the basing OUTCOME is scored once, under R15, and cited here): `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started > 0` on ENG; type-2 projects queued and completing (capacity actually grows); the ENG planes/capacity ratio IMPROVES vs the `31eaf7e6` baseline of 3.5× at 1944.6 (capacity tracks fleet growth — R23 funding + R26 lane are the mechanisms under test here); USAAF-in-UK cited from R15's scoring.
- **Probe:** `var ENG "^wa_ai_uk_air_dbg" <saves...>`; `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_building_type"`; USA wing basing via the top-level `strategic_air` block (wings → `air_base` id → `state=`; see R12 note). **Counter caveats (registry: `documentation/WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §2.1):** `_best = -2` means "no deficit / satisfied" — health, not breakage. `_capacity`'s ×100 is correct (`AIRBASE_CAPACITY_MULT = 100` in this mod).
- **Fix 46/47 probes (added 2026-08-12) — run all five; the funding ones are what distinguish "the fix worked" from "the fix locked a builder out".**
  1. **`_started` semantics changed — do not compare the number across the Fix 47 boundary.** It now counts projects that verifiably entered the queue (queue-growth guard, matching the `thair` twin); before, it counted deficit pulses and over-read ~4× (`9a4cd657` 1944.7: ENG 97 + USA 39 = 136 against 34 real levels; `911bed3c`: 176 against ~47). **A post-fix campaign reading much LOWER is the fix working, not a regression.** Still cross-check against `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves...> --match air_base`.
  2. **USA not locked out** — `var USA "^wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned"` must be **non-zero at the majority of samples from the first deficit onward**, and `var USA "^wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started"` must keep climbing. Zero USA air factories across a whole deficit window = the host-gate regression returned. Baseline to beat: USA supplied 585 of 874 air civ-months over 1942–44 on `9a4cd657`.
  3. **ENG funds it too, without losing its refineries** — `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_air_factories_assigned"` should sit near 33–40 (was **1** at `9a4cd657` 1944.7), while ENG still shows live type-8/11/15 refinery projects. Air *and* refineries is the point of the override; air *instead of* refineries means the override did not apply.
  4. **The override is live and self-releasing** — `flags ENG --match WA_AI_PC_override_max_factories_factor` present while a deficit stands, and `var ENG "^wa_ai_pc_override_max_factories_factor"` = **0.5**. Any value other than 0.5 means it is fighting the railway system, which writes the same shared flag/variable pair (`railway_core.txt:200-201`). Absent after the target is met = correct (30-day expiry).
  5. **No cross-builder double-booking (Fix 46)** — dump both PC queues at 2–3 dates and confirm **no state id appears as a type-2 target in ENG's and USA's queue at the same date**. `9a4cd657` 1944.7 had states 126 and 860 in both.
  6. **Slot budgets are un-shared (lever 2)** — for ENG and USA, read `wa_ai_pc_type_id^<id>` alongside `wa_ai_pc_building_type^<id>` for every queued project. A country may now hold **up to 3 type-2 projects tagged 20 (UK) AND up to 3 tagged 21 (theatre) simultaneously** — that combination is the fix working, not over-queueing. Every type-2 project should carry 20, 21, or 0 (island fortification); a type-2 tagged **13 or 14 means a stale railway id leaked** and the caller-side retirement failed. Baseline that must not recur: at `911bed3c` 1943.6 both of ENG's air slots were Pacific on the pulse the UK deficit reopened.
- **Capacity expectation for the next run:** with ENG ~33 and USA ~60 civs on UK air (vs 41 combined measured), hosting-state levels should climb from the 119 recorded at `9a4cd657` toward the 132 ceiling. **Reaching the ceiling is a real stop condition, not a failure** — at that point the state list or `@UK_AIR_TARGET_LEVEL_CAP` is the constraint, and *neither should be touched before the basing outcome (R15) justifies more capacity.*
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural — the wing-basing outcome is the point; the dbg variable alone is not a pass).
- **Streak:** 0
- **Metric notes (2026-08-11, both corrections — an earlier version of this file got both wrong):**
  - `wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_capacity` = (hosting-state `air_base` levels + ROOT-queued type-2 projects targeting a hosting state) × 100. **The ×100 is CORRECT for this mod** — `common/defines/05_defines.lua:173` sets `AIRBASE_CAPACITY_MULT = 100` (vanilla is 200). There is no "2× pessimistic" adjustment to make; the dbg figure *is* the engine figure.
  - **`wa_ai_uk_air_dbg_started` is NOT a project count — do not read it as throughput.** `strategies.txt:1120-1128` increments it unconditionally after `WA_AI_PC_start_project`, which silently declines on `queue_max`/dedup; the theatre twin has the queue-growth guard (`:1440-1451`), the UK one does not. Proof: Δ`started` = Δ`called` = 13 exactly in every full deficit quarter — one "start" per weekly pulse, impossible when ≤3 projects exist and each takes 35–45 days. Across the campaign ENG 115 + USA 61 = 176 recorded starts produced **+50 actual levels (≤28%)**. Read it as "pulses spent in deficit with an eligible state".
  - `dbg_best` = **−2 when there is no deficit**, otherwise the ladder level a project was started at. A `−2` is the system saying *satisfied*, not *broken*.
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-10 · `66d6b53c` · PASSED — `started` 22→74→102 from 1942.6 (engages exactly when planes overshoot capacity — the pre-1942 `best=-2` is correct gating); type-2 projects at Cornwall/Warwickshire/Gloucestershire; those states' air-base levels flat for 6 years then step 1–2 → 7–8; hosting capacity 3 400 → 8 500. **Caveat:** the USAAF barely used it — 3 wings / 500 planes on UK soil pre-D-Day vs 8 595 planes parked in CONUS. Construction leg is proven; consider a numeric wing bar for the basing leg (engine wing placement, not the WA project system).
  - 2026-08-11 · `31eaf7e6` · FAILED — the construction leg is alive (`started` 13→52→83, capacity 3.9k→7.1k, ENG home air bases reach level 8–9) but the basing outcome — the point of the item — is absent: ZERO USA planes based in the UK at 1943.6 and a single 98-plane wing at 1944.6.1 (pre-D-Day), worse than `66d6b53c`'s 500. Second defect: ENG planes/capacity worsens to 3.5× at 1944.6 (+1.4k capacity added against +13.2k planes).
  - 2026-08-11 · code audit — the basing gap is R15's root cause (see R15: net-negative Allied air ledger; between the fall of France and 1944.5.15 NO positive-importance region is reachable from an English airfield, so the USAAF has no reason to cross the Atlantic — the single wing appears in exactly `Allies_dday_air`'s 6-week window). The 3.5× ratio is NOT a stale input: the target formula tracks fleet correctly (faction planes × 0.5) but is throttled by `queue_max = 3` × 1 level/project and structurally capped at 11 states × level 12 = 13 200 capacity — already reached vs a ~25k-plane faction. Real accounting bug found: the queue scan (`strategies.txt:970-974`) counts ANY type-2 project as UK hosting, including theatre-air-base projects elsewhere (bounded +300). Do not chase the capacity leg further until the demand ledger (R15 fix A/B) ships.

Ratio **improves to 2.91** vs the 3.5× baseline — the capacity criterion is met. (Ignore the "1.52 on a 200/level convention" figure from the first write-up: this mod's `AIRBASE_CAPACITY_MULT` is 100, so 2.91 is the real number.) **Outcome: still absent.** ZERO USA planes in UK states at 1943.6; at 1944.6 a single 300-plane wing in **Lanark (Scotland)** and nothing in the southern/eastern England bomber counties — marginally better than `31eaf7e6`'s 98-plane wing, still not a build-up. Do not ship more capacity until R15's demand side is understood.
  - 2026-08-11 · quantitative audit of the capacity leg (campaign `911bed3c`) — **the constraint is THROUGHPUT, and the earlier "structural ceiling already reached" verdict is REFUTED.** Target formula confirmed: `0.5 × (ROOT + faction allies) deployed planes` vs `100 × (hosting-state levels + ROOT-queued UK type-2)`, reproducing `dbg_best` at **14/14 sample dates**. The system was not confused — at 1943.6 it correctly saw no deficit (target 4 984 vs capacity 5 000, 16 planes of slack); the deficit reopened 1943.9 and peaked at **31.9 levels short** at 1944.6; it closed in 1945 because the *fleet shrank* (20 386 → 13 473 planes), not because the ladder ran out. Ceiling usage: peak **87 of 132 levels = 65.9%**, highest single state 9 of a cap of 12 — the ceiling never bound. Throughput arithmetic: one project = exactly one level (`core.txt:989`), cost `500 + 250 × level`, ≈35–45 days at a full 20-factory slot ⇒ ~8–10 levels/yr/slot. Achieved 17 levels (1943.6→1944.6) and 18 (1944.6→1945.6) ⇒ **≈2.0 continuously funded slots, against demand growing 52.1 levels/yr — 3.1× short.** Three causes, all measured: **(a)** `queue_max = 3` is a country-wide *type-2* budget shared with R16's theatre strategy (`core.txt:1124-1129`, `:1181-1183`) — at 1943.6, the moment the deficit reopened, **both of ENG's air-base slots held Pacific theatre projects**, and at 1945.6 all three USA slots were non-UK; **(b)** `_project_priority = 100` (`strategies.txt:1116`) is the bottom band, below refineries at 250 and rail at 500/1000 — at 1944.6 ENG held three UK air-base projects **all at 0 factories** (stall 4/5/11 weeks) behind a 20-factory prio-250 refinery, and ENG's `pc_air_factories_assigned` reads 0 in 11 of 15 samples; ENG's state-860 project took ~22 months for a 35-day level, a **19× slowdown**; **(c)** ENG's own 29–44 factory pool funds 1–2 slots at best — essentially all real UK construction came from USA via `allied_build`. **Even the design's theoretical maximum (6 funded slots = 51.6 levels/yr) is below peak demand growth (52.1).** Levers, in order of measured effect: (1) lift `_project_priority` out of the bottom band while a deficit is open — biggest win, smallest edit, but it is a shared path and costs R25's refineries the top ENG slot; (2) make the UK strategy's `queue_max` count only hosting-state type-2 projects, un-sharing it from R16; (3) only then raise `queue_max` above 3. **Do not touch the level cap or the state list — neither binds.** Also confirmed: the prior audit's "queue scan counts ANY type-2" bug is **FIXED** in this build (`strategies.txt:1054-1066`, via `506d670de`) and verified numerically at 4 dates.

London/Norfolk/Kent/Cornwall, **with USA allied-building Essex/Norfolk/Kent** — Fix 37 visibly working). Outcome: USAAF in the UK is **0 at 1943.6 and 1943.9**, then **600 at 1944.3** (Yorkshire 300 + Cornwall 300), 600 at 1944.4, 400 at 1944.5, **300 at 1944.6** — versus 500 / 98 / 300 in the three prior campaigns. Still 4–5% of a 12–13k USAAF, and sited north of the Midlands rather than in the bomber counties.

  - **Submarine hypothesis — TESTED AND FALSIFIED as the cause of the US army cap (2026-08-12).** The German submarine campaign is real and enormous — **9 284 convoys sunk** 1939–1946.6 (peak 1 779 in 1945, never suppressed, 100–190/month in the final months), with **~101 of GER's 102 naval factories on `ger_cruiser_submarine_hull_3` for six years ≈ 12 % of all German industry**, 486 submarines built against 4 capitals and 11 screens, 287 boats at peak against ~329 cumulative losses. It nonetheless cannot be what capped the US army: **ENG absorbed 1.3–1.9× more convoy losses than the USA in every rolling window and still grew 12 → 152 divisions**; USA convoy stock rose **monotonically** 805 → 1 932 → 3 718 → 5 623 → 7 750, where 7 750 is the mod's own scripted cap; USA manpower **never dipped**, sitting welded at 319 000 for 17 consecutive months and 440 000 for 10, including months that lost 12 divisions; and USA infantry equipment **accumulated to 130 376** while the army was pinned at 51. Victim split of the 9 284: ENG 3 630, SOV 2 542, USA 1 393, CAN 702. **Two probe limits worth carrying forward:** the save contains **no in-transit loss accounting of any kind** (no manpower-lost-at-sea field, no destroyed-transport counter; `division.killed` is cumulative *combat* manpower), so "were troops sunk" is only answerable through the monthly division series; and `sunk_convoys_history` is a **rolling 24-month window per save**, so full-campaign coverage needs stitching across ~5 saves.
  - **Two independent findings surfaced by that test, neither owned by this item:** (a) **ENG and USA produce ZERO convoys in the entire campaign** (`equipment_production.convoy = 0`, no convoy line in `naval_lines` at any anchor) — all Allied tonnage is the 1936 stock plus the `z_WA_ai_USA.txt` / `z_WA_ai_ENG.txt` top-up decisions, so **convoy stock measures the script cap, not the Atlantic battle**, and ENG runs a chronic deficit of 13 034 required vs 4 021 held. (b) **GER holds ~100 factories on submarine lines but builds only 10 boats between 1945.6 and 1946.7** (cumulative 476 → 486) — stalled lines rather than de-prioritisation, and very likely the same iron/steel starvation R29 records for GER in that window (steel park 20 active / 43 inactive at 1946.4, iron net −240). Both deserve their own items if they recur.
  - **Casualty-exchange reading to treat with caution:** the `war_relation` ledger reads USA 3.31 M cumulative casualties by 1946.7 and an adverse exchange of roughly 13:1 against GER (2.89 M suffered vs 214 k inflicted), with ENG at a similar ratio. **Do not act on that number yet** — `war_relation` blocks are stored on only one side of each pair (a known asymmetry logged during the R13/R17 scoring), so "inflicted" may not mean what it appears to. No instrumentation to retire - the probe is save-native.*

*__The "3 decks with no fighter wing" constant that held this item for one session is now fully explained, and it is NOT an AI defect.__ Named at last, from a two-pass `strategic_air` join on `02bd4445`: JAP decks **391 Ryujo, 392 Kaga, 393 Soryu, 394 Akagi, 395 Hosho**; USA decks **396 USS Ranger, 397 USS Lexington, 398 USS Saratoga**. Worked example, IJN Kaga at 1944.6 - `air_base` id 392, `carrier={ id=2072 }`, capacity 90, **nine `cv_nav_bomber` wings (ids 261-269, count=10 each) = 90/90 planes = 100.0% fill, cv_fighter 0**; at 1946.1 the same nine wing ids are still there, re-equipped within the archetype to `JAP_b6n_airframe` but **never re-roled** in ten game-years. Root cause is the **start-state OOB, not the AI**: `history/units/JAP_1936_air_bba_ai.txt` defines Kaga as nine consecutive `JAP_b4y_airframe` wings with no fighter wing at all (Ryujo 4, Hosho 2, Akagi 9, Soryu 7 the same), and `history/units/USA_1936_air_bba_ai.txt` does the same with `USA_bg_airframe` for Ranger/Lexington/Saratoga. **The BBA-branch OOB files contain ZERO carrier-fighter entries; their `_legacy` twins do contain them.** The decisive contrast: at 1938.6 the two carriers JAP had *built itself* (decks 430 and 433, both Hiryu) already carried **40 cv_fighter each** - the AI composes every hull it produces on a 4:3 fighter/bomber split and simply never revisits the ones it was handed. Material was never the constraint: 167 carrier fighters in JAP stock and **3 200 more in land-based wings** at 1946.1 (2 114 of them A6M-family), and 71 in stock as early as 1938.6.*

*__Correction of record, and it inverts a claim this item carried: "fill ~100%, zero empty decks" is true for JAP and FALSE for the USA.__ JAP is exactly at capacity - 1 380 / 1 380 across all 20 decks at both 1944.6 and 1946.1. The USA at 1944.6 is **2 019 / 2 720 = 74.2%**, because **11 Essex-class decks sit at exactly 50/100**, each carrying five `cv_nav_bomber` wings with `count = 0` - **55 empty wings**, though no deck is wholly empty. The cause is a shortage, not an assignment failure: **14 `cv_nav_bomber` airframes in USA stock at 1944.6**. Aggregating fill across decks is what hid this for five campaigns; any future carrier probe must report per-deck, not per-country. Also worth recording: **zero `cv_cas` planes exist on any JAP or USA deck at any sampled date** - the `cv_small_bomber_airframe` archetype is entirely unused afloat.*

*__Two defects handed off, neither of them what this item tested__ (protocol: a pass that reveals a new defect does not get absorbed into the item). (i) **The BBA carrier OOB gap** - eight start-state hulls across JAP and USA with no fighter wing, stable for ten game-years; it is start-state data, small and verifiable in the first save of any campaign. (ii) **The USA carrier naval-bomber shortage** - 11 half-empty decks against 14 airframes in stock. Note (ii) points the opposite way from the carrier-aircraft **over**-production finding recorded against JAP/ENG/FRA in the anomalies list below: Japan holds 740 naval bombers afloat and ~7 000 carrier aircraft parked ashore while the USA cannot fill its decks, so **any global cut to carrier-aircraft production would hit the one navy that is short**. Whoever picks up either defect must read both together.*

### R15. Air forces deploy to contested theatres

- **Fix under test:** `edb746d17` (generic Default-layer AIR domain: 10 theatre blocks — enemy AND own side both hold anchor states; capital-theatre excluded) + `124b2a3b6` (pulls raised +10k→+50k AND the ENG.txt Allied suppression family retuned −250k…−1M → −2k…−40k, making the Allied European ledger net-positive in every contested region — worst case Poland/296 = +2k; this removes the `31eaf7e6` root cause). Watch item from the retune: +50k exceeds the engine's hot-front ~35k reference — air abandoning an active non-capital home front for a foreign contested theatre would be the over-correction signature.
- **Pass:** by 1943.6 USA has ≥25% of deployed planes based outside the continental US, with USA wings at bases in NA/Mediterranean states (446/447/452, 458–460, 115/117/156) or Pacific island states (629 Hawaii, 634 Solomons, 633 Marshalls, 639/643/725 Micronesia, 1001–1004 New Guinea, 327/623–628 Philippines). Secondary: ENG's home-based RAF share does not increase vs the `66d6b53c` baseline; GER gains air presence over a contested Normandy post-landing. Baseline to beat: USA 62% CONUS (8 595/13 791).
- **Probe:** walk the top-level `strategic_air` block — wings → `air_base` id → `state=` — classify CONUS vs overseas per wing; count per theatre. (Carrier decks are the `air_base` blocks without `state=` — exclude them from this count.)
- **Launch-validation note:** two parse-risk constructs need the boot test (F9) before the next campaign: `has_deployed_air_force_size` without `type`, and `region = <id>` inside `capital_scope`. If the size trigger proves type-mandatory, drop that gate line.
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural — basing shares over time).
- **Streak:** 0
- **History:**
  - 2026-08-10 · `2b607968` (local, fix loaded mid-campaign 1943.3, probed 1943.7.15) · FAILED with caveats — USA at 71.7% CONUS (worse than the 62% baseline), **zero** USA planes in the contested Italy/Med theatre; ENG's 30% Med deployment is real but attributable to pre-existing ENG.txt specials; GER 70% east by engine combat terms, zero over Italy. Caveats: only 2.5 game-months since load; migration IS live (1 000-plane Newfoundland→Azores→Madeira ferry chain, ENG wings on Sicily 5 days post-landing); Med bases possibly RAF-saturated. **Suspected binding gap: no friendly in-range air-base capacity — the air-base-construction follow-up is the dependency to test next.** Re-score on a from-start campaign before concluding the pull values are wrong.

Quebec, Nova Scotia, Avalon), counted as overseas but functionally CONUS — excluding them drops 1943.6 to **~7.9%**. The `124b2a3b6` retune is in this build; it did not move the needle. Dalmatia, 33 Centre-Sud) — no home-soil spree.*

***Instrumentation disposition — PROMOTE, do not delete, and this is a deliberate departure from the default.** The `WA_AI_thair_dbg_*` family fails the standing-vs-probe test in `documentation/WA_TLM_TELEMETRY_SYSTEM.md` §3.8 on the "was it used beyond this item's own pass/fail" clause: the triple `called` / `started` / `active` is the **only** way any of the four defects below is visible, all four are still open, and `_started` additionally serves as the documented reference implementation of the queue-growth guard that R8's `uk_air` twin lacks (§2.1). Deleting it would make four live defects undiagnosable and remove the only correct counter of its class. Decision: **keep the write sites, promote the family to a standing `WA_TLM_thair_*` name with its own registry row**, as a separate commit with a boot test — the same disposition and the same reasoning R14's `_active` received, and for the same reason it was not executed inline (it edits construction-priority gameplay files immediately before a campaign, where a parse error costs a multi-hour cloud run for no analytical gain). The item is retired now; the rename is owed.*

***Four unresolved defects carried out of R16 — they are NOT fixed, they simply were never this item's pass criterion.** (1) **The JAP builder is dead**: 434 calls, 4 starts, `active` = 0 from 1943.6, so the Axis Pacific side builds nothing all war — 4th campaign running. (2) **The IPP Philippine group 623-628 is still flat** (623 = 1, 624-628 = 0) for the 5th campaign; Fix 38's host-is-funding gate demonstrably does not reach occupied-subject territory. (3) **327 Manila reaches air-base level 5, above the 4-cap**, at 1944.8 and holds it — third instance of the pattern, and the subject-owned-state ladder-exclusion hypothesis is now the standing explanation. (4) The overproduction guard is breached again at **CAN 32 vs USA 58 = 55%**, which is not the "small fraction" the guard expects; the guard has never once failed an item in five campaigns, so give it a numeric bound or drop it. Any of these deserves its own item if it is to be fixed.*

### R17. Italian campaign: Sicily held, mainland reached, Axis defends

- **Architecture review: `documentation/WA_ITALIAN_THEATRE_REVIEW.md` (2026-08-12). Read it before proposing any fix here, and before re-scoring this item.** Headline: **the Allies are not beaten in Italy, they stand down — and winning is what switches them off.** Every Allied boost in the theatre is gated on the *enemy* holding ground and every suppression on the *Allies* holding it, so capturing Calabria (state 156) swings `area_priority` italy −600 / south_italy −800 in one instant and permanently arms `no_italy_invasion_early` (whose third OR branch has no exit condition). The Italian defection then fires an Allied brake (−80) and a German boost (+100/+100) on the *same two regions*. Confirmed fingerprint: the theatre becomes British — USA 0-4 divisions vs ENG 26-36 through 1945. **Three things the review RULES OUT — do not re-investigate:** AIFC/schwerpunkt selection (measured and refuted — USA anchored in Italy 10 consecutive months, ENG 22 of 32 saves), `CAPS_remote_fronts_*` (Italy is in no sink area), and the `fall_achse` buffer (its own gate makes it dead within one day on the defection path — **criterion (4) below has been crediting it for a front stack it never supplied**).

- **Fix under test (2026-08-12, NOT YET TESTED — the gate-inversion package, §10 of the review):** P1 removed the unbounded third `enable` branch of `ALLIES_no_italy_invasion_early_THEATRE` (the permanent `area_priority italy -200` that armed on holding Sicily + Calabria); P2 added `ALLIES_italy_mainland_push_{THEATRE,FRONT}`, the successor the bridge never had, mutually exclusive with it; P3 scoped both `avoid_italy_overstack` brakes to `is_western_allies_major`, gave them the principle-4 escape hatch (`WA_AI_MILITARY_owns_italy_mainland`) so co-belligerent ITA stops braking its own soil, and dropped the `has_government = democratic` gate; P4 re-homed `ENG_war_against_ITA_3_FRONT` to the Faction layer as `ALLIES_italy_cobelligerent_support_FRONT` so USA/CAN get the only post-flip Italian positive in the mod. INVASION domain deliberately untouched (anti-scatter + Balkan policy ride on it). **Sub-probe, no new instrumentation:** (a) Allied divisions in regions 21/23/238 must NOT fall across the month Calabria (156) flips Allied — the `9a4cd657` signature is 73 → 45 with France still at 0; (b) USA divisions in Italy after the Italian defection must clear a floor of ~10 (observed 0-4 across 1944.10–1945.6, against ENG's 26-36) — the ENG/USA ratio is the single cleanest fingerprint that P4 took; (c) co-belligerent ITA divisions in regions 21/23 must not decline monotonically after the flip (observed 44 → 32 → 13 → 9-11). Score all three before judging the criteria below.
- **Fix under test:** `f99217c39` (hold-Sicily bridge blocks, USA suppression window, `fall_achse` flag fix, ITA home buffer 0.15). **Husky size dependency:** the landing is 12 ENG + 8 USA = 20 divisions per Fix 33 (R18), NOT the 40 that `76dde84ed` briefly produced — criterion (1) was recalibrated accordingly.
- **Pass** (criteria 1 and 3 recalibrated 2026-08-11 per the `31eaf7e6` scoring — monthly saves cannot observe "two weeks after" and Avalanche fires 1943.9.9): next campaign with a successful Husky: (1) Allied divisions on Sicily (115+1032) ≥ 12 at the SECOND monthly snapshot after both states flip, Sicily never recaptured; (2) USA divisions present in region 238 during the bridge window (suppressions inert in-window); (3) USA divisions on the Italian mainland by **1943.10**; (4) once GER sets `GER_fall_achse_prepared` (not at war with ITA), GER divisions in northern Italy (158/162/159/161/160/736/856/157/2) > 0 within 60 days — **mark NOT CHECKED (vacuous) if ITA never capitulates**, as in `31eaf7e6`; (5) ITA pre-invasion garrison on 115/1032/117/156 lighter than the `2b607968` baseline (9 divisions across the four states at 1943.7.1) but non-zero.
- **Probe:** state controllers + division locations for the listed states across 1943.7–1943.10 saves; GER flag `GER_fall_achse_prepared` set-date; division-location counts per the province lists in `history/states/`.
- **Watch item (regression risk from the fix):** the reactivated fall_achse buffers let GER park up to 0.2 army-fraction in N. Italy while fighting SOV — watch eastern-front density; and the 0.15 buffer also lightens Campania/Calabria (Avalanche zone), single shared ratio.
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural campaign outcome).

### R19. Starved priority-construction projects age out; no factories burn in hostile states (Fix 34)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 34 on `ai-rework` (`WA_AI_CONSTRUCTION_PRIORITY_core.txt`). Two parts: (a) the stall sweep's `queue^num > 5` gate replaced with `WA_AI_PC_assigned_factories_total > 0` — a project starved of factories for 25+ weeks is now cancelled whenever factories were flowing to *other* projects, regardless of queue length; (b) `WA_AI_PC_assign_factories` skips projects whose target state is hostile-controlled (same controller test as the completion path), freezing their progress instead of burning civ-weeks toward a completion that spawns nothing.
- **Bug being fixed:** `2b607968` @ 1943.9.1: ROM queue `[6,1,7,0]` — naval base slot 7 (prio 5000, 3000/3000, stall 17w) and air base slot 0 (prio 100, target state 143 no longer ROM-controlled, stall 17w, ~140 civ-days already burned) both starved behind prio-9999 railway segments, with the sweep unreachable at queue=4 ≤ 5. The old gate also allowed 5 starved nonrail projects to lock every `< 5` strategy gate shut permanently.
- **Pass:** on any campaign save carrying the fix, mid/late war: (1) no AI country has a queued project with `wa_ai_pc_stall_weeks^id > 26` while its `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories_total > 0`; (2) no queued project whose target state is enemy-controlled has `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories^id > 0`; (3) negative guard — a country with `assigned_factories_total = 0` (collapse/full occupation) keeps its stalled queue uncancelled: stall_weeks > 26 with total = 0 is correct behaviour there, not a failure; (4) railway construction still completes normally — **criterion recut 2026-08-11 for Fix 41:** cancellations now come from TWO legitimate sources: the week-25 sweep AND the overtake lane's revalidation (from ~week 12, counted in `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels`). A cancel at 12-24 weeks is only a defect if it is NOT accounted for by the reval counter; spot-check `sweep-cancels = total cancels − reval_cancels` were genuinely starved 25+ weeks.
- **Probe:** extract `wa_ai_pc_queue`, `wa_ai_pc_stall_weeks`, `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories`, `wa_ai_pc_assigned_factories_total`, `wa_ai_pc_target_state` for all majors plus 2–3 occupied/front-line minors (ROM is the reference case); cross-check target-state controllers in the save's `states={}` block.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — direct variable observation, no behavioural judgement).
- **RETIREMENT HELD at 3/3 (2026-08-12) — do not delete this item without resolving two probe defects first.** (a) **Criterion (4) has never been computable from a save.** Only `wa_tlm_pc_aging_reval_cancels` and `wa_tlm_pc_aging_grants` exist; there is no total-cancel counter, so `sweep-cancels = total − reval` cannot be evaluated — add the total counter or drop the arithmetic from the criterion. (b) **The synchronised stall-counter reset recurs for the third campaign and is now unambiguous:** at 1945.6, SOV shows **14 slots all at exactly 19**, JAP **17 slots all at exactly 23**, GER **9 slots all at exactly 16** (`9a4cd657` had SOV 13@13, JAP 27@14). That is a queue-wide reset, not per-project aging — which means criterion (1)'s "no funded project ages past 26 weeks" may be an artefact of the reset rather than the fix working, i.e. the mechanism under test may not be running at all. Per `../SKILL.md`, a caveat of that shape is the difference between PASSED and NOT CHECKED, so the streak stands but the item does not retire. (c) Separately, **criterion (2) is not discriminating as written** — it tests "is now hostile-controlled", not "was funded while hostile", so a capitulated country's frozen queue (FRA slot 6, funded 1940.7 while FRA still held state 15) trips it forever. Recut it to compare the funding date against the flip date, or exempt countries whose PC block is unchanged since capitulation.
- **Streak:** 4
- **History:**
  - NOT YET TESTED — fix postdates every campaign in the registry.
