### R30. SOV builds ocean-going submarines, not coastal ones (Fix 46)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 46 — `common/ai_equipment/SOV_naval.txt`, `SOV_naval_submarine` design-priority re-ordering (commit hash TBD; authored 2026-08-12, uncommitted at the time this item was written). Root cause: the group's priority ladder was written as an ascending "higher hull index = better" ramp (10 / 60 / 120 / 140) but the `sov_submarine_hull_N` numbering is class-historical, not a quality order — `hull_3` is the **coastal Malyutka** (`naval_range` 1640, `max_strength` 6, `build_cost_ic` 100). SOV starts with `sov_submarine_3` researched (`history/countries/SOV - Soviet Union.txt:152`) and `sov_submarine_4` (Whiskey) is a 1948 tech, so from 1936.1.1 the coastal boat was permanently the highest-priority *reachable* design for the `naval_submarine` role — the role that carries ~100% of SOV's naval output (base ratio 25 + `WA_DEFAULT_production_navy_main_focus_on_submarines` +75, with screens and light cruisers cancelled to 0). Fix 46 promotes `submarine_default` (Shchuka, `hull_1`, range 3820 / str 14 / cost 260) to 120, demotes `submarine_3` to 5, and adds `ship_radar` to the Shchuka `allowed_modules` so the new mainstay can fit the radar slot its hull already has. Research was **checked and exonerated**: `WA_AI_RESEARCH_needs_submarines` is true for SOV (`WA_AI_RESEARCH_naval.txt:110-114`) and every reachable hull tech is a 1936 start tech, so no `ai_will_do` change was needed.
- **Pass, two legs (both must hold):**
  1. **Design leg (the fix itself).** SOV's running `naval_submarine` production line(s) sit on a variant whose hull is `sov_submarine_hull_1` (Shchuka) — or `hull_2`/`hull_4` — and **zero factories** on a `sov_submarine_hull_3` (Malyutka) variant, at every save from the first one that has SOV naval lines at all. Any factory on a Malyutka variant is an outright FAIL.
  2. **Fleet leg (the outcome).** SOV's Malyutka hull count does not grow beyond its 1936 OOB baseline of **49** (`history/units/SOV_1936_naval_mtg.txt`; attrition may take it below), while the Shchuka count grows well past its baseline of **39** — expect Shchuka to be the largest single SOV submarine class by 1942 and to keep climbing.
- **Probe:** save-native, **no WA_TLM needed** — production lines and fleet composition are both directly readable. (a) Design leg: dump SOV's `production` section (`savegame.py section SOV production <saves…>`); lines identify equipment by numeric `equipment_variant_index`, so resolve the ids through the top-level `equipments={}` registry exactly as R11 does — SOV submarine variants resolve to names derived from `sov_submarine_hull_1` … `sov_submarine_hull_4`. **A name grep on `production` alone returns MIO idea names, not equipment — resolve through the registry or the answer is wrong.** (b) Fleet leg: count SOV ships by hull in the country's fleet/navy blocks, grouping on the same registry-resolved variant names; report Shchuka / Pravda / Malyutka counts per save. Baselines to compare against: 39 Shchuka, 3 Pravda, 49 Malyutka at 1936.1.1. (c) Build fingerprint: the design leg *is* the fingerprint (pre-fix builds put the line on `hull_3`, post-fix on `hull_1`), so confirm the build by git ancestry of the fix commit before recording a FAILED; a secondary confirmation once SOV has radar tech is that the Shchuka variant carries a `ship_radar` module, which no pre-Fix-46 Shchuka design could produce.
- **Result of the Fix 51 sub-probe, and a hard constraint on how it can be read (2026-08-13):** on `02bd4445` the SOV line **never left `hull_3`**, so the lever produced no observable effect. But **`production_upgrade_desire_offset` is not serialized into a savegame's `ai` section** — verified with three ungated controls including the long-standing `SOV_dont_build_shit_guns`, all equally invisible — so a save can tell you the *outcome* and can never tell you whether the block armed. Do not record "Fix 51 did not arm"; record "Fix 51 changed nothing observable". The distinction decides whether the next step is debugging the trigger or abandoning the lever, and only an isolated in-game test can separate them.
- **Fix 51 sub-probe (2026-08-12, THE decisive experiment for this whole family):** `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_SOV.txt` adds `production_upgrade_desire_offset` `sov_submarine_hull_1 = +100` / `sov_submarine_hull_3 = -100`. This is the **production-line** layer, the one Fixes 46/48/50 missed. Score exactly as the design leg above: does any SOV `naval_submarine` line leave `hull_3`? **SOV is the decisive case because research suppression is impossible here** (`sov_submarine_3` is a 1936 start tech), so this reads the lever in isolation. If SOV moves off hull_3 the lever works and `tools/equipment_evaluator/`'s emit stage is re-targeted onto it; if it does not, the contextual-equipment approach needs a different mechanism entirely and that file is deleted. No new instrumentation — same save-native reading.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — the pass criterion is a discrete design-selection fact, "which hull is the line on", not an emergent front outcome; the fleet leg is arithmetic on the same reading).
- **Adjacent, NOT under test here (RESOLVED 2026-08-12, now scored by R33):** the same inverted ladder existed in `JAP_naval.txt` (live) and `GER_naval.txt` (inert) and was left alone by Fix 46, which was scoped to SOV. **Fix 48 closed both** — see **R33**. Score JAP and GER submarine hull selection there, not here; R30 remains SOV-only.
- **Streak:** 0
- **Status:** FAILED
- **History:**
  - *(none yet)*
  - 2026-08-12 · `9a4cd657` · **NOT YET TESTED (build predates Fix 46) — recorded as the pre-fix CONTROL reading, and it convicts the defect.** Build proof: `wa_tlm_version = 2` on SOV/ENG, no `wa_tlm_r47_*` family at all, and **no variant name containing "long range" in any of 31 sampled `equipments={}` registries** (3 567 → 7 307 entries each). Pre-fix behaviour: SOV's `naval_submarine` line sits on **`sov_submarine_hull_3` (Malyutka, coastal) at every save 1938.6 → 1946.3** — 1–3 concurrent lines, 1–23 factories, and **zero factories ever on `hull_1`/`hull_2`/`hull_4`**. Fleet: Shchuka **39 → 39 flat**, Pravda 3 → 3 flat, Malyutka **49 → 224 (1939.6) → 393 (1941.6) → 531 (1943.6) → 655 (1945.6) → 803 (1946.7)** — ~95% of the submarine fleet and, unlike `9a4cd657` (peak 634 then war losses to 189), **it never stops climbing**. Design leg **FAIL**: every SOV submarine line in every sampled save (1936.2, 1938.6, 1940.6, 1942.6, 1944.6, 1946.4 plus seven intermediates) sits on the `sov_submarine_hull_3` Malyutka variant (id 988) — 1 to 10 factories, up to five concurrent lines — with **zero factories ever** on hull_1/2/4, despite Shchuka promoted to 120 and Malyutka demoted to 5. Fleet leg **FAIL**: Malyutka **49 -> 151 -> 310 -> 486 -> 620 -> 702** (14x), Shchuka pinned at its 39 OOB baseline (38 by 1944.12), Pravda static at 3. **Mechanism (corrected 2026-08-12):** the fix edited the **design** layer when the failure is in the **production-line** layer — see R35's ROOT CAUSE bullet. `sov_submarine_hull_1`/`_2` carry `obsolete=yes` from 1943 while `hull_3` does not, but that is the consequence of the line having converted, not the reason priority failed. **SOV is the hardest of the five cases**: `sov_submarine_3` is a 1936 START tech (`history/countries/SOV - Soviet union.txt:152`), so research suppression cannot help; the candidate levers are `production_upgrade_desire_offset` at a negative value on the Malyutka type, or removing the start tech. Do **not** re-tune the priority factors.

Separating "block never armed" from "block armed and the engine ignored it" now requires an isolated in-game test; it cannot be read out of any campaign. Streak stays 0.

### R31. SOV keeps a ≥1000 km multirole fighter across the La-5FN generation (Fix 47)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 47 — `common/scripted_triggers/WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_triggers.txt` + `common/scripted_effects/WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_effects.txt` (new generic context-trigger family and its monthly latching effect), consumed by `common/ai_equipment/SOV_planes.txt`, group `SOV_fighter_mr` (commit hash TBD; authored 2026-08-12, uncommitted when this item was written). Root cause: every design in every `ai_equipment` group is killed by a blanket `has_tech = <next tech> → factor = 0`, so the AI always builds the newest researched airframe regardless of context. Computed `air_range` for the SOV multirole chain, model `(airframe base + Σ module add_stats) × (1 + Σ module multiply_stats)`: La-5 **1166 km** → La-5FN **836 km** → La-7 **1364 km**. The La-5FN generation is a 330 km regression that sits 164 km under the 1000 km target for ~1 year of the Eastern Front. Fix 47 adds `fighter_mr_7_lr` (same `SOV_la_5fn_airframe`, `internal_slot_1 = fuel_tanks_medium`, bomb rack removed → **1017 km**), demotes the short-range `fighter_mr_7` to 0.1 and retains `fighter_mr_6` (La-5) at 0.5 as the fallback — all three only while the latched `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_needs_long_range_air` holds.
- **Pass, three legs (all must hold):**
  1. **Latch leg.** SOV's `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n` is > 0 by the first save after SOV enters its main land war, and `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_last_t` keeps advancing (the latch persists, it does not fire once). SOV trips the "deep theatre" leg on `num_of_controlled_states > 79` the moment it is at war, so a zero here means the latch or its wiring is broken, not that the situation never arose.
  2. **Design leg.** From the save after SOV researches `sov_fighter_multirole_ad_tech_7` (La-5FN) until it researches `..._tech_8` (La-7), SOV's `air_fighter_mr` production line sits on a variant whose airframe is `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` **with `fuel_tanks_medium`** (the long-range variant, named "Lavochkin La-5FN (long range)") **or** on `SOV_la_5_airframe` (the retained La-5). A line on a `SOV_la_5fn_airframe` variant carrying `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small` during that window is the pre-fix behaviour and is a FAIL.
  3. **No-thrash leg.** Across the campaign SOV's `air_fighter_mr` line changes variant at most ~3 times in the La-5/La-5FN/La-7 window. More than that means the latch is chattering and the hysteresis is not doing its job — report the flip dates.
- **Probe:** (a) Latch leg: `savegame.py tlm SOV <last-save>` and read `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n`, `_first_t`, `_last_t`; decode the clock as `year = 1936 + floor(clock/12)`. **Absence contract:** no `wa_tlm_*` namespace at all = pre-WA_TLM build, probe VOID; `wa_tlm_version` present but `< 3` = build predates Fix 47, mark **NOT YET TESTED**, never FAILED. `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n = 0` **with** `wa_tlm_version = 3` is a real zero reading and scores the latch leg FAILED. (b) Design leg: dump SOV's `production` section (`savegame.py section SOV production <saves…>`); lines identify equipment by numeric `equipment_variant_index`, so resolve the ids through the top-level `equipments={}` registry exactly as R11 and R30 do — **a name grep on `production` alone returns MIO idea names, not equipment; resolve through the registry or the answer is wrong.** Report the resolved variant name and its modules per save from 1942 to campaign end. (c) No-thrash leg: the same per-save variant series, counted for changes. (d) Build fingerprint: `wa_tlm_version = 3` plus git ancestry of the Fix 47 commit; confirm before recording any FAILED.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — the pass criterion is a discrete design-selection fact, "which variant is the line on", plus a counter reading; it is not an emergent front outcome).
- **KNOWN UNKNOWN carried by this item:** nobody has yet validated in-game how often the engine re-evaluates `ai_equipment` `priority` modifiers, or whether scripted triggers resolve inside them at all (no `ai_equipment` file in the repo used one before Fix 47). If the design leg reads pre-fix behaviour while `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n > 0`, the latch is working and the **priority modifiers are not being re-evaluated (or the scripted trigger is not resolving)** — that is a different, larger finding than "the gate is mistuned", and it invalidates the whole contextual-switching approach, not just this pilot. Say which of the two it is before proposing a fix.
- **Streak:** 0
- **Status:** FAILED
- **History:**
  - *(none yet)*
  - 2026-08-12 · `9a4cd657` · **NOT YET TESTED (latch leg VOID — no r47 instrumentation in the build)** — pre-fix control reading. `wa_tlm_version = 2`; `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_*` absent entirely. Pre-fix behaviour: SOV's `air_fighter_mr` line runs `SOV_la_5_airframe`[`self_sealing_fuel_tanks_large`] 1942.6–1943.5 → **`SOV_la_5fn_airframe`[`self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small`] 1943.6–1944.1 (8 months, 76–85 factories)** → `SOV_la_7_airframe`[`self_sealing_fuel_tanks_large`] 1944.2–1946.3. That middle window is exactly the documented 836 km regression, with the short-range `internal_slot_1` and no "(long range)" variant in the registry. No-thrash leg reads **2 variant changes** (1943.5→6, 1944.1→2), inside the ~3 budget — so the hysteresis question is already answered favourably on the *pre-fix* baseline.
  - **The KNOWN UNKNOWN does NOT come into play, and remains entirely open.** Its precondition is pre-fix design behaviour *while* `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n > 0`; here the metric does not exist, so the latch never ran and neither reading ("gate mistuned" vs "`ai_equipment` priority modifiers are not re-evaluated / the scripted trigger does not resolve") is supported or excluded. **This is the single most important question the next campaign must answer**, because Fix 49 doubled the exposure to it — if the mechanism does not work, R31 and R32 fail together for one shared reason and the whole contextual-switching approach needs rethinking, not retuning.

### R32. ENG's strategic-bomber aluminium step waits for headroom — in BOTH bomber groups (Fixes 47 + 49)

- **Fix under test:** two changes, ONE item — the gate is only meaningful as a *role* cap, so it is scored as one.
  - **Fix 47** — same trigger/effect family as R31, consumed by `common/ai_equipment/ENG_planes.txt`, group `ENG_heavy_strategic_bomber` (commit hash TBD; authored 2026-08-12, uncommitted when this item was written). Measured per-unit resource step at the Manchester → Lancaster transition (`eng_heavy_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_3`, `start_year 1942`, taken 1943-44 in practice): Manchester Mk IA = **5 aluminium / 5 coal / 2 rubber, ~45.4 IC**; Lancaster B Mk I = **7 aluminium / 5 coal / 2 rubber, ~58.1 IC** — **+2 aluminium (+40%) and +12.7 IC (+28%) per unit**, charged per assigned military factory per day, i.e. **+40…+80 aluminium/day** on the 20-40 factories ENG runs on heavy bombers. Fix 47 gates `heavy_strategic_bomber_3` and `_3_1` (the whole Lancaster generation, because `..._tech_3_1` follows within months) on `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_can_absorb_aluminium_shock_large`, and defers `heavy_strategic_bomber_2`'s (Manchester) supersession until the gate opens. `heavy_strategic_bomber_4` (Lincoln, 1945) is deliberately ungated as the escape valve.
  - **Fix 49** (2026-08-12, same file, group `ENG_strategic_bomber`) — closes the bypass Fix 47 knowingly left open (this item's old "scope limitation" bullet). Both groups serve `air_strategic_bomber`, and this one reaches 7 aluminium/unit **two years earlier**, ungated, so the Fix 47 gate capped nothing. Verified per-unit costs (archetype `large_bomber_airframe` contributes aluminium 1 / coal 5 / rubber 2 — checked to be **identical** to `large_heavy_bomber_airframe`, so the two groups' figures are directly comparable): Hampden Mk I **5 Al, ~31.1 IC, 2482 km** → Stirling B Mk I **7 Al, ~59.6 IC, 950 km** → Halifax B Mk II **7 Al, ~56.4 IC, 1778 km** → Halifax B Mk III **7 Al, ~62.0 IC, 2036 km** → Windsor B Mk II **7 Al, ~62.4 IC, 3340 km**. The step is Hampden → Stirling (`eng_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_2`, `start_year 1940`): **+2 aluminium (+40%) and +28.5 IC (+92%)**, and it is purely the 2-engine → 4-engine module change (2 Al → 4 Al), the same mechanism as Manchester → Lancaster. Fix 49 therefore gates the **whole 7-aluminium generation** — `strategic_bomber_2` (Stirling), `_3` and `_4` (both Halifaxes) — on the same latch, defers `strategic_bomber_1`'s (Hampden) supersession until the gate opens, hard-cuts the Hampden at `eng_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_5`, and leaves `strategic_bomber_5` (Windsor, 1946) ungated as the escape valve. It also adds `strategic_bomber_2_lr` ("Short Stirling B Mk I (long range)", `internal_slot_1` rear MG → `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small`, **950 → 1038 km**, same 7 aluminium) behind *both* the aluminium gate and `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_needs_long_range_air`, because the Stirling generation is also a range collapse under the 1000 km bar. **No new instrumentation:** Fix 49 reuses the Fix 47 latches, so `wa_tlm_version` stays at 3 and the gate leg below is unchanged.
- **Correction of record carried by this item:** the reported symptom was "steel demand roughly doubles". **Plane designs in this mod cost aluminium, coal and rubber — not steel.** The only plane modules with a steel `build_cost_resources` are the `*_engine`-suffixed heavy-armour variants, which no ENG bomber design uses. Score this item on aluminium; a steel reading is not evidence either way.
- **Pass, four legs (all must hold):**
  1. **Gate leg.** ENG's `wa_tlm_r47_alu_large_n` and `_first_t`/`_last_t` are consistent with its net aluminium: the gate is open only in months where the net balance clears roughly +50/day. If `n = 0` all campaign, ENG never had the headroom — that is a **PASS on the gate's own terms** and pushes the verdict onto legs 2-3.
  2. **Role leg (Fix 49, the one that makes the cap real).** While the gate has never opened (`wa_tlm_r47_alu_large_n` still 0) and neither escape tech is in (`eng_heavy_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_4` for the heavy group, `eng_strategic_bomber_ad_tech_5` for the other), **no** ENG `air_strategic_bomber` production line — in either group — sits on a 7-aluminium airframe. The 7-Al set is `ENG_lancaster_airframe`, `ENG_stirling_airframe`, `ENG_halifax_airframe`. The permitted set in that window is `ENG_whitley_airframe`, `ENG_manchester_airframe`, `ENG_hampden_airframe` (5 Al each) and the older `ENG_heyford_airframe`. A Stirling or Halifax line before the first gate-open month is an outright FAIL, and it is the *same* failure a pre-fix Lancaster line would be — do not score it as a separate finding.
  3. **Escape-valve leg.** Once the escape tech of a group is researched, that group's line moves onto its ungated modern airframe — `ENG_lincoln_airframe` (heavy group, 1945) / `ENG_windsor_airframe` (other group, 1946) — **even if the gate never opened**. A group left with no producing line at all, or still on the 1938-40 fallback after its escape tech, means a gate closed with no way out and is a FAIL of the arrangement rather than of the economy.
  4. **Economy leg (the point of the fix).** ENG's aluminium park does not repeat the `911bed3c` collapse in the window where the bomber transitions would otherwise have landed: `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves…> --match aluminium` must not show active levels falling toward 0 with 10+ inactive levels across 1943-1945. *(Shared boundary with R29: if the collapse happens anyway and `r47_alu_large_n = 0`, the bomber transitions were not the cause and this leg is `NOT CHECKED`, not FAILED — say so explicitly rather than double-counting R29's failure here.)*
- **Probe:** (a) Gate leg: `savegame.py tlm ENG <last-save>` for `wa_tlm_r47_alu_large_n`, `_first_t`, `_last_t`, decoded as `year = 1936 + floor(clock/12)`; cross-check against `savegame.py resources ENG <saves…>` for aluminium — read the **EFFECTIVE** column (`to_use[0] + to_use[2]`, what `resource@X` actually compares). **Neither the `produced` line alone** (that mistake nearly produced a false FAIL on R25) **nor the `net` column alone** (that mistake produced a false accusation against Fix 39, retracted in this item's history on 2026-08-13 — ENG 1942.6 is net 807.3 but effective +8.3 against a `> 50` bar). **Absence contract:** no `wa_tlm_*` at all = probe VOID; `wa_tlm_version < 3` = **NOT YET TESTED**, never FAILED; `n = 0` with `wa_tlm_version = 3` is a real zero. (b) Role and escape-valve legs (one reading, two questions): `savegame.py section ENG production <saves…>`, resolving `equipment_variant_index` through the top-level `equipments={}` registry (a name grep on `production` returns MIO idea names, not equipment); report **every** ENG bomber line's airframe per save, **1940 → end** (not 1942 → end: the Stirling window opens in 1940, which is where Fix 49's half of the item lives). Both groups feed the same role, so the reading is per-line, not per-group. (c) Long-range sub-check, report-only: when a line does sit on `ENG_stirling_airframe` while `wa_tlm_r47_lrange_n > 0`, say whether its `internal_slot_1` is `self_sealing_fuel_tanks_small` (the Fix 49 long-range variant, "Short Stirling B Mk I (long range)") or the stock rear MG. This is evidence for R31's KNOWN UNKNOWN about whether `ai_equipment` priority modifiers are re-evaluated at all, not a pass criterion here. (d) Economy leg: `savegame.py buildings ENG <saves…> --match aluminium` (the command sums the `*_inactive` twins; a state block omits `controller=` when controller = owner, which the command already handles).
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — a counter reading plus a discrete "which airframe is the line on" fact).
- **Build fingerprint:** `wa_tlm_version = 3` proves Fix 47 only — **Fix 49 shipped no new instrumentation and is invisible to TLM.** Confirm the Fix 49 half by git ancestry of its commit before recording any FAILED on legs 2-3. Save-native corroboration, if available: the string "Short Stirling B Mk I (long range)" can only appear in the `equipments={}` registry of a post-Fix-49 build (absence proves nothing — the variant is only created if it was ever selected).
- **Scope limitation from the original item — now CLOSED, do not re-diagnose:** this item used to record that the parallel `ENG_strategic_bomber` group was ungated and could serve the same role at 7 aluminium/unit from 1940, making the Fix 47 gate a non-cap. **Fix 49 closed it** (see above), and the item was widened rather than split, because two gates on one role are one behaviour. If a scoring session finds the bypass alive, the finding is "Fix 49 did not take", not a new scope limitation.

### R33. JAP builds ocean-going submarines, not coastal ones (Fix 48)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 48 — `common/ai_equipment/JAP_naval.txt` (`JAP_naval_submarine`, live) and `common/ai_equipment/GER_naval.txt` (`GER_naval_submarine`, inert), design-priority re-ordering (commit hash TBD; authored 2026-08-12, uncommitted when this item was written). Same defect class as Fix 46/R30: the group's priority ladder was written as an ascending "higher hull index = better" ramp, but the `<tag>_submarine_hull_N` numbering is class-historical, not a quality order. JAP hull table (`common/units/equipment/ship_hull_submarine.txt:1991-2423`): `hull_1` Ro 4840 km / str 20 / 420 IC (start tech), `hull_2` Kaidai 5820 / 36 / 1160 (start tech), `hull_3` Otsu **7500** / 52 / 1700 (1937), `hull_4` Hei **7500** / 52 / 1700 + a fourth torpedo slot (1938), `hull_5` Ro-101 **COASTAL 2580** / 12 / 240 (1941), `hull_6` Ha-201 **COASTAL 2320** / 8 / 120 (1944). The old ladder was 10 / 60 / 70 / 70 / **80** / **90**, so the two coastal hulls topped the group from 1941 and 1944 respectively. JAP carries the base `naval_submarine` role_ratio of 25 (`WA_AI_PRODUCTION_DEFAULT_navy.txt:7`) with **no cancelling profile**, so unlike GER this ladder really drives production. Fix 48 re-orders to 10 / 60 / **120** / **140** / **5** / **3** (Hei > Otsu > Kaidai > Ro >> coastal) and adds `ship_radar` to `submarine_2`'s `allowed_modules`, closing the same unfillable-radar-slot gap Fix 46 closed on the SOV Shchuka (`jap_submarine_hull_2` has a `fixed_ship_radar_slot` at `ship_hull_submarine.txt:2067`). The GER half demotes `ger_submarine_hull_5` (coastal Type XXIII, 2080 km / str 4 / **560 IC**, dominated by every other GER hull) from **150 to 3**.
- **Module-mountability audit, done and clean (do not redo):** every JAP `naval_submarine` design's `target_variant` modules were checked against its hull's `module_slots.allowed_module_categories`. All six are mountable — the impossible-module bug found on the SOV Malyutka does **not** recur here. (That SOV bug is still open and is *not* part of Fix 48: `SOV_naval.txt` `submarine_3` requests `sub_2_torpedo_tube` on `sov_submarine_hull_3`, whose torpedo slots accept only `sub_torpedo_tube` / `ship_mine_layer_sub` — `ship_hull_submarine.txt:2008-2020`. Now low-impact because Fix 46 demoted that design to 5, but if a scoring session sees a Malyutka line producing nothing, that is the reason.)
- **Pass, two legs (both must hold):**
  1. **Design leg (the fix itself).** JAP's running `naval_submarine` production line(s) sit on a variant whose hull is `jap_submarine_hull_4` (Hei) or `hull_3` (Otsu) once those techs are in — `hull_1`/`hull_2` before 1937 — and **zero factories** on a `jap_submarine_hull_5` or `hull_6` variant at any save. Any factory on a coastal-hull variant is an outright FAIL. The 1941-1945 window is the one that matters: that is where the pre-fix build flips.
  2. **Fleet leg (the outcome).** JAP's `hull_5` + `hull_6` counts stay at or near **0** (1936 OOB has none of either — `history/units/JAP_1936_naval.txt` is 21 × `hull_1`, 27 × `hull_2`, plus 8 cruiser subs), while `hull_3`/`hull_4` counts grow from 0 through the war. Expect Otsu/Hei to be the largest JAP submarine classes by 1943.
- **Probe:** save-native, **no WA_TLM needed** — production lines and fleet composition are both directly readable. (a) Design leg: dump JAP's `production` section (`savegame.py section JAP production <saves…>`); lines identify equipment by numeric `equipment_variant_index`, so resolve the ids through the top-level `equipments={}` registry exactly as R11 and R30 do — JAP submarine variants resolve to names derived from `jap_submarine_hull_1` … `jap_submarine_hull_6`. **A name grep on `production` alone returns MIO idea names, not equipment — resolve through the registry or the answer is wrong.** Report the resolved hull per line per save, 1940 → campaign end. (b) Fleet leg: count JAP ships by hull in the country's fleet/navy blocks, grouping on the same registry-resolved variant names; baselines to compare against are 21 Ro, 27 Kaidai, 0 Otsu, 0 Hei, 0 Ro-101, 0 Ha-201 at 1936.1.1. (c) Build fingerprint: the design leg *is* the fingerprint (pre-fix builds put the line on `hull_5` from 1941 and `hull_6` from 1944, post-fix on `hull_4`), so confirm the build by git ancestry of the Fix 48 commit before recording a FAILED.
- **GER leg — do NOT score as a pass/fail:** GER's half of Fix 48 is preventive. `WA_AI_CONFIG_focus_on_cruiser_submarines` (`WA_AI_CONFIG.txt:336`) routes GER to the `cruiser_submarine` role and `WA_DEFAULT_production_navy_main_focus_on_cruiser_submarines` drives GER's `naval_submarine` ratio to 0 (base 25 − 25), so **nothing should select out of `GER_naval_submarine` at all**. If a scoring session ever sees a GER `naval_submarine` line, that is a finding about the *routing*, not about this item — open a new item. Record it as an observation in this item's evidence line and move on.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — the pass criterion is a discrete design-selection fact, "which hull is the line on", not an emergent front outcome; the fleet leg is arithmetic on the same reading). Retire together with R30 if both clear, and note in the retirement that the durable rule — *`ai_equipment` priority ladders must be capability-ordered, because `<tag>_<class>_hull_N` numbering is class-historical* — belongs in `wa-lessons-learned`, not here.

  - 2026-08-13 · `02bd4445` · **FAILED — same shape as `bec4d829`, and the coastal flip now arrives EARLIER and runs longer.** Build IN (see R30); checked on **all 120 saves**. Design leg **FAIL**: the single JAP `naval_submarine` line climbs correctly `jap_submarine_hull_2` (1936.2–1938.7) → `hull_3` Otsu (1938.8) → `hull_4` Hei (1939.9–1942.10), then **flips to coastal `hull_5` Ro-101 at 1942.11** — one to two months earlier than `bec4d829` (1942.12) and `a8b29e09` (1943.1) — and on to coastal **`hull_6` Ha-201 from 1944.5 through 1946.1**. That is a **live coastal line for the last 39 months of the campaign, with non-zero factories in 36 of them**, against a ladder Fix 48 re-ordered to 10/60/120/**140**/**5**/**3**. Fleet leg **FAIL** (weak signal as predicted, and the reason to score this item on the design leg): h5 reaches 3 while h3/h4 reach only 1/3, and Kaidai (29) + Ro (17) still dominate at campaign end; `hull_6` has delivered **zero** hulls in 21 months at ~1 factory. The 1936 baseline reproduces exactly (21/27/0/0/0/0), which independently validates the registry id→definition resolution used for both legs. **GER observation (not scored, per this item's instruction):** GER runs `naval_submarine` lines (`ger_submarine_hull_1`/`_2`) **only in 1936.2–1936.7** — the historical starting queue — and from 1936.8 to campaign end every GER submarine line is `ger_cruiser_submarine_hull_1`…`_4` (516 line-instances on `hull_3` alone, up to twelve 10-factory lines in parallel by 1945). Routing works; GER's half of Fix 48 remains correctly preventive. Nothing to open. Streak stays 0.

### R34. The Allies can still invade Europe after December 1944 (Normandy gate fix)

- **Fix under test:** the `ALLIES_dday_hold` branch-2 repair (2026-08-12, `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_MILITARY_FACTION_ALLIES_INVASION.txt`). Root cause: branch 2 of the block's `enable` was the bare pair `date > 1944.12.1` AND `has_war_with = JAP`, with **no exit condition of any kind**. From December 1944 onward, with Japan still in the war, every western major was permanently subject to `invade GER -2000`, `invade FRA -2000`, `invade RFR -2000` and `invasion_unit_request tag = GER -100`. In `9a4cd657` the Allied presence in metropolitan France went **69 divisions (1944.9) → 0 (1944.10)** and was never re-attempted across the remaining 18 months while GER held 23-25 divisions there — this suppression was armed for all of it. Note branch 4 (Allies hold nine French states) is **not** the defect: the block carries `abort_when_not_enabled = yes`, so branch 4 self-releases when the ground is lost. The fix pairs branch 2 with `GER = { surrender_progress > 0.5 }`, mirroring the situational exit `ALLIES_dday_fire_FRONT` already received for the identical construct ("switched off all France invasion execution in any campaign where the European war ran long"). The `surrender_progress` term reads the **enemy's** collapse, not own capability, so it is not the R27 anti-pattern.
- **Pass, two legs:**
  1. **Gate leg.** In any campaign reaching 1945 with GER uncapitulated and below 0.5 surrender progress, the Allies retain invasion capability: at least one western major shows a live naval-invasion plan or an actual landing against GER/FRA/RFR-held European coast after 1944.12.1. A campaign where GER *is* above 0.5 scores this `N/A` — the suppression is then correct.
  2. **Outcome leg.** Allied divisions in metropolitan France do not sit at **0 for more than 3 consecutive months** while GER is uncapitulated and the Allies hold ≥1 European foothold elsewhere. The `9a4cd657` reading is 18 consecutive months at zero.
- **Probe:** divisions-per-state walk over the 24 metropolitan French states per month from the D-Day month to campaign end (the method used for the review's §3.2 table — `units`-scoped, cross-checked against `savegame.py army`); plus GER `surrender_progress` at each sample to decide whether leg 1 is scoreable or `N/A`. No new instrumentation — both legs are save-native.
- **Threshold:** 5 (behavioural — whether a theatre stays re-enterable over the back half of a campaign is an outcome, not a variable reading).
- **Streak:** 3
- **Status:** PASSED
- **History:**
  - *(none yet)*
  - 2026-08-12 · `a8b29e09` · **PASSED (both legs) — the Normandy lockout is gone.** Leg 2 PASS: Allied divisions in the 24 metropolitan French states **never return to 0** after the July-1944 landing — 59 (1944.7) → 87 (1944.12) → 84–89 through 1945 → 93 (1945.12) → 59 (1946.7), holding 10–11 of 24 states throughout, against `9a4cd657`'s 69→0 at 1944.10 and 18 months at zero. Leg 1 PASS and scoreable: GER is far below 0.5 surrender progress in 1945 (36 of 37 core states still GER-held at 1945.6), and live western-major naval-invasion plans are created **after 1944.12.1** against Axis-held European coast — ENG→Peloponnese (186, RGR-held) created 1944.12.16, USA→N. Sicily (115, ITA-held) created 1945.4.29 **and again** 1945.7.12, ENG→Sardinia (114) created 1946.6.28. **Important qualifier for the next scoring: plan creation is unblocked, execution is not.** No European landing actually executes after 1944.12 — the only invasion that fires is ENG's against JAP (Jan 1945, `_adds` 5→6 with `_active` 1), USA's `_adds` is frozen at 11 from 1944.9 to 1946.7, and the USA Sicily order created 1945.7.12 is still listed unchanged in the final 1946.7 save. ENG's Aquitaine plan (19, GER-controlled, created 1944.8.1) is also never purged. The gate this item tests is open; **whatever now blocks execution is a different defect and needs its own item if it recurs.**
  - 2026-08-12 · `bec4d829` · **PASSED (both legs) — the Normandy lockout stays gone on its second test.** Leg 2 **PASS**: Allied divisions in the 24 metropolitan French states, sampled monthly 1944.6 -> 1946.4, read 2 / 54 / 21 / 8 / 4 / 4 / 5 / 4 / 5 / 9 / 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 / 10 / 7 / **0 (1945.11)** / 1 / 3 / 3 / 4 / 3 — **one single zero month**, no run anywhere near the 3-month bar, against `9a4cd657`'s 69 -> 0 and 18 consecutive months at zero. The Allies hold **9 of 24** states continuously 1944.8-1945.10 and **24 of 24** from 1945.11. Leg 1 **PASS and scoreable**: GER is uncapitulated and far below 0.5 collapse (18 of 65 GER-owned states enemy-held at 1945.1, 20 at 1945.6), and **no `value=-2000` invade suppression exists on ENG or USA at 1945.1 or 1945.6** — branch 2 is demonstrably not armed; ENG carries 4 `recently_invaded_areas` entries at 1945.1. **Qualifier carried forward:** every naval-invasion plan *created* after 1944.12.1 in the sampled saves is Pacific-directed (USA 1945.5.31 New Guinea, ENG 1945.10.4 West Bengal); ENG's live European-coast plans stage from Corsica (created 1944.7.15) and Dodecanese (1944.11.3), both pre-window. Leg 1 therefore rests on the absent suppression plus a never-lost foothold rather than on a fresh European plan. **Probe correction for the next scoring: `surrender_progress` is NOT stored in the save** on any country at any date — this item's probe line asks for it as if it were save-native. Score it through a VP/state-share proxy instead, and note `victory_points` is a multi-line block that a single-line parse reads as 0.
  - 2026-08-13 · `02bd4445` · **PASSED (both legs).** Leg 2 **PASS**: Allied divisions in the 24 metropolitan French states, monthly 1944.6→1946.1, read 26/77/97/115/117/111/128/137/121/140/148/125/137/143/138/127/59/61/39/56 — **never zero, not one zero month**, against `9a4cd657`'s 18 consecutive months at zero; Allied-controlled French states peak 21/24 (1945.4-1945.8). Leg 1 **PASS and scoreable** on the state-share proxy: GER is uncapitulated with only **19 of 63 owned states enemy-held at 1945.1 and 16 of 63 at 1945.6**, far below 0.5, and **no `value=-2000` invade suppression on ENG or USA at 1945.1 or 1945.6**. **And unlike the last two campaigns a European landing actually EXECUTES after the window opens: ENG lands on province 11735 = state 736 Istria on 1945.5.26**, with USA logging repeated naval-invasion attempts through 1945.4-1945.5 — the `a8b29e09` qualifier ("plan creation is unblocked, execution is not") is **discharged**. **Note for the reader: this item passing while the western front collapses anyway is not a contradiction** — R34 tests whether the theatre stays re-enterable; F5's failure is the posture cliff, a different gate (see R37).

### R35. Parallel tank branches select the best researched and affordable design (Fix 50)

- **Fix under test:** Fix 50 — competitive-frontier generation for 11 branched
  tank roles in `ENG/FRA/GER/JAP/SOV/USA_tank.txt` (authored 2026-08-12,
  commit `88e516780`). The selector derives topology from technology `path` edges,
  ranks every design sharing the role, neutralises old file-order supersession
  hooks, and keeps lower positive-priority fallbacks. Resource-heavy ranks are
  held by the latched `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_can_absorb_*_shock_*` gates. The canonical
  regression is USA medium tanks: the real branches are Sherman and T20/T23,
  not `Sherman -> T20 -> T23`; the generated order is **M4A3E8 > M4A2 > T23 >
  T20 > M4A1 > M3 Lee > M2**.
- **Pass, three legs:** (1) after USA has researched M4A2 and T20/T23, its
  `land_medium_tank` production line resolves to M4A2 or M4A3E8, never T20/T23;
  (2) with `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_3` researched, an open
  `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_tun_small_ok` latch selects M4A3E8, while a closed latch
  exposes M4A2 rather than leaving the role empty; (3) across the other ten
  branched groups, every active line resolves to the highest-ranked researched
  design whose resource hold is open, or to a lower/emergency fallback — zero
  branched roles with researched designs may have no production candidate.
- **Probe:** save-native; no WA_TLM added. For USA plus ENG/FRA/GER/JAP/SOV,
  dump each country's `production` and technology/flag sections at 1940.6,
  1942.6, 1944.6 and campaign end. Resolve every `equipment_variant_index`
  through the top-level `equipments={}` registry (a name grep on `production`
  alone returns MIO names and is invalid). Compare the resolved chassis/design
  against `tools/equipment_evaluator/output/parallel_branch_decisions.md`, but
  first filter that ranking to researched enable techs and discard any rank
  whose required `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_*_ok` country flag is absent. Explicitly
  report USA's medium line, `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_tun_small_ok`, and whether
  `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_2`, `_3_3`, `_4`, `_5` are researched at each
  date. Build fingerprint: git ancestry must include Fix 50; generated
  `WA_EQUIPGEN_BEGIN ... kind=priority_factor` comments do not serialize into
  saves and are not a save-native fingerprint.
- **Fix 51 sub-probe (2026-08-12) — the whole tank package moved to the production-line layer.** `tools/equipment_evaluator --domain tanks --apply-production-strategy` now generates `common/ai_strategy/WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_<TAG>_TANKS.txt` for ENG/FRA/GER/JAP/SOV/USA: **57 `production_upgrade_desire_offset` entries over all 11 frontiers**, `+100` on the wanted design and `-100` on every newer chassis, with the Fix 47 resource latches carried across as the block's `enable`. Emission is proved exhaustively (`test_generation.py::ProductionStrategyTests`): every reachable gate state names exactly one wanted design, and **no gate state leaves a branched role empty** — which is leg (3) satisfied by construction rather than by observation. USA medium is the canonical row: `+100` on `_4_2` (M4A3E8) while `WA_AI_EQUIPMENT_can_absorb_tungsten_shock_small` holds, `+100` on `_4` (M4A2) while it does not, `-100` on `_5` (T20) and `_6` (T23) in both states. Score legs (1) and (2) unchanged; also check SOV medium (was T 43, expected T 34 85) and USA modern (was M46, expected T26E4). **Mind the numbering: the tech and equipment tokens are numbered differently** (M4A2 = tech `usa_medium_tank_chassis_3_2` but equipment `tank_usa_medium_chassis_4`). Score legs (1) and (2) unchanged. Note the latch was CLOSED on USA at 1944.6 and 1946.4 on `bec4d829`, so the M4A3E8 branch may stay unobserved again — that is a real reading, not a probe gap. Also re-check the two script bugs fixed alongside: `SOV_heavy_tanks` no longer emits scientific notation (`factor = 9e-06`, which PDXScript reads as **9**, had inverted the T 35 emergency floor above IS 8/IS 4/IS 3 — generator fixed in `tools/equipment_evaluator/emit.py::_pdx_num`), and ENG `medium_tank_4` (Crusader Mk I) now builds `tank_eng_medium_chassis_3` instead of the Covenanter's `_2`.
- **Comet sub-probe (Fix 55, 2026-08-13) — and a RETRACTION of one of this item's own faulty frontiers.** `tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` (Comet) had **no design in any ENG group**, so the AI auto-designed it and ran it with 30+ factories from 1944.6 to the end of `02bd4445`, outside the ranking, the resource gates and the emitted offsets. `ENG_tank.txt` now carries `medium_tank_8` (Comet) — modules taken from the chassis' own `default_modules` and audited slot by slot against `allowed_module_categories` (9/9 mountable, 4/4 required slots filled), deliberately **without** a supersession modifier, because what `eng_medium_tank_chassis_5` leads to is `eng_modern_tank_chassis_1`, a *different role*; hooking the Comet to it would empty ENG's medium role the moment modern tanks land. **The retraction:** with the Comet finally visible, the evaluator ranks it **PRIMARY, rank 8 of 8, above the Cromwell (+1.224 vs +1.157)**. So this item's "ENG medium 1944.6 & 1946.4 Cromwell (100) → **Comet**" faulty-frontier line was a **false accusation — the AI's choice was correct all along**, and the defect was that the mod could not see the design it was choosing. Do not re-score ENG medium as a selection failure. `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_ENG_TANKS.txt` consequently went from a lone `+100` on the Cromwell to a real two-state selector (Comet while the steel+tungsten latches hold, Cromwell with `-100` on the Comet when they do not), which also closes the "emits only ONE block and no negative offsets" generator defect — by fixing the emitter's *input*, not the emitter. **Pass criterion:** ENG's `land_medium_tank` line resolves to `tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` or `_4_2` and the role is never empty; score it on the design leg like the rest of this item. **Still open in the same role, deliberately not done:** `tank_eng_medium_chassis_7` (M3 Grant), `_8` (Sherman III) and `_9` (Sherman V) remain uncovered — ENG ran 15 factories on `_9` at 1943.6 in this campaign. They are lend-lease leaf techs with no `start_year` and no `leads_to_tech`, i.e. a parallel supply-driven set rather than a succession step, so adding them changes the frontier's topology and is a separate judgement.
- **Ladder-rescale sub-probe (2026-08-13, uncommitted at the time of writing).** The generated priority ladder underflowed on deep frontiers — `SOV_heavy_tanks` emitted `factor = 0.000009`, which fixed-point script numbers cannot tell from `0`, i.e. *never build this design* rather than *emergency fallback*. A configurable floor (`config.json` `frontier_priority.floor = 0.1`, matching vanilla's own smallest `ai_equipment` factor; Expert AI never goes below 0.5) now re-spaces only the underflowing tail; every rank above the knee keeps its exact geometric value. **25 factors rescaled across `FRA/GER/JAP/SOV/USA_tank.txt`** — the deepest, `SOV_heavy_tanks`, goes `10 / 3 / 1 / 0.3 / 0.000009…` → `10 / 3 / 1 / 0.3 / 0.266 / 0.235 / 0.208 / 0.184 / 0.163 / 0.144 / 0.128 / 0.113 / 0.1`. **Pass criterion:** in any campaign where a branched role's better ranks are all shut out (resource latch closed, or the chassis not yet researched), the role still runs *a* line rather than none. That is R35 leg 3 and needs no extra probe — score it there. **Known trade-off to watch, and the reason this is a sub-probe rather than a silent change:** the re-spaced band is flat (all of `SOV_heavy_tanks` ranks 1–9 now sit within 2.7× of each other instead of spanning four orders of magnitude), so if the engine treats priority as a weighted lottery, a country reduced to its emergency floor will pick among old heavies less sharply by rank than before. If a campaign shows a country building, say, a T-35 while an IS-3 is available and unheld, **lower the floor to 0.01 rather than reverting** — it is one config value and the top of every ladder is unaffected.
- **Coverage sub-probe (2026-08-13) — check this BEFORE scoring a leg, it can void one.**
  `ai_equipment` only steers equipment it has a design for; a chassis unlocked inside a role
  with no design in any of that country's groups is auto-designed by the engine and is
  invisible to the ranking, the resource gates and the emitted offsets. The evaluator now
  reports these (`python -m equipment_evaluator --domain tanks`, written to
  `tools/equipment_evaluator/output/coverage_gaps.md`): **39 gaps, 11 in branched roles**,
  including all four post-Cromwell ENG mediums (`tank_eng_medium_chassis_5` Comet, `_7`,
  `_8`, `_9`), `tank_ger_modern_chassis_5`, `tank_usa_modern_chassis_5`/`_6`,
  `tank_usa_heavy_chassis_6`, `tank_sov_heavy_chassis_2_3`. **If a save shows a line on an
  uncovered chassis, that role is NOT a fix failure — it is a design-layer hole**, and it is
  the reason `WA_AI_PRODUCTION_COUNTRY_ENG_TANKS.txt` emits a lone `+100` on the Cromwell
  with no suppressions. Report which faulty frontiers are coverage gaps and which are real
  selection failures; they need different fixes and must not be pooled.
- **Threshold:** 3 (narrow — the result is a discrete equipment-variant choice
  after resolving tech and latch state, not an emergent campaign outcome).
- **Streak:** 0
- **Status:** FAILED
- **History:**
  - *(none yet)*
